  "content": "# Containerized Claude Code Runtime Implementation Plan\n\n> **For Claude:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task.\n\n**Goal:** Run each Claude Code session inside a gVisor-sandboxed Docker container (\"sidecar\") for code execution sandboxing, multi-tenant isolation, and defense in depth.\n\n**Architecture:** A lightweight TypeScript HTTP server inside a Docker container wraps the Claude Agent SDK. The host FastAPI backend manages container lifecycle via Docker SDK for Python. DuckDB stays on the host; the sidecar reaches it via MCP over the host network. A feature flag (`CONTAINER_ENABLED`) allows fallback to the existing subprocess model.\n\n**Tech Stack:** TypeScript (sidecar server), Python (container manager), Docker SDK for Python (`docker` package), gVisor (`runsc` runtime), Express.js (sidecar HTTP framework)\n\n---\n\n### Task 1: Sidecar TypeScript project scaffolding\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `sidecar/package.json`\n- Create: `sidecar/tsconfig.json`\n- Create: `sidecar/src/types.ts`\n\n**Step 1: Create `sidecar/package.json`**\n\n```json\n{\n  \"name\": \"duckdb-agent-sidecar\",\n  \"version\": \"1.0.0\",\n  \"private\": true,\n  \"scripts\": {\n    \"build\": \"tsc\",\n    \"start\": \"node dist/server.js\",\n    \"dev\": \"tsx src/server.ts\"\n  },\n  \"dependencies\": {\n    \"express\": \"^4.21.0\",\n    \"claude-agent-sdk\": \"^0.1.38\"\n  },\n  \"devDependencies\": {\n    \"@types/express\": \"^5.0.0\",\n    \"@types/node\": \"^22.0.0\",\n    \"tsx\": \"^4.0.0\",\n    \"typescript\": \"^5.7.0\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**Step 2: Create `sidecar/tsconfig.json`**\n\n```json\n{\n  \"compilerOptions\": {\n    \"target\": \"ES2022\",\n    \"module\": \"NodeNext\",\n    \"moduleResolution\": \"NodeNext\",\n    \"outDir\": \"dist\",\n    \"rootDir\": \"src\",\n    \"strict\": true,\n    \"esModuleInterop\": true,\n    \"skipLibCheck\": true,\n    \"declaration\": true\n  },\n  \"include\": [\"src\"]\n}\n```\n\n**Step 3: Create `sidecar/src/types.ts`**\n\n```typescript\nexport interface QueryRequest {\n  message: string;\n  session_id?: string;\n  system_prompt: string;\n  model?: string;\n  mcp_server_url?: string;\n}\n\nexport interface HealthResponse {\n  status: \"ok\" | \"error\";\n  message?: string;\n}\n```\n\n**Step 4: Install dependencies**\n\nRun: `cd sidecar && npm install`\nExpected: `node_modules/` created, `package-lock.json` generated.\n\n**Step 5: Verify TypeScript compiles**\n\nRun: `cd sidecar && npx tsc --noEmit`\nExpected: No errors.\n\n**Step 6: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add sidecar/package.json sidecar/package-lock.json sidecar/tsconfig.json sidecar/src/types.ts\ngit commit -m \"feat(sidecar): scaffold TypeScript project for agent sidecar\"\n```\n\n---\n\n### Task 2: Sidecar HTTP server implementation\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `sidecar/src/server.ts`\n\n**Step 1: Write `sidecar/src/server.ts`**\n\nThis is the thin HTTP wrapper around the Claude Agent SDK. It exposes three endpoints:\n\n- `GET /health` — readiness probe\n- `POST /query` — accept a query, stream SSE response\n- `POST /stop` — gracefully stop current session\n\n```typescript\nimport express, { Request, Response } from \"express\";\nimport { ClaudeSDKClient, ClaudeAgentOptions } from \"claude-agent-sdk\";\nimport type { QueryRequest, HealthResponse } from \"./types.js\";\n\nconst app = express();\napp.use(express.json());\n\nconst PORT = parseInt(process.env.PORT || \"3000\", 10);\n\n// Track active client for /stop endpoint\nlet activeClient: ClaudeSDKClient | null = null;\n\napp.get(\"/health\", (_req: Request, res: Response) => {\n  const response: HealthResponse = { status: \"ok\" };\n  res.json(response);\n});\n\napp.post(\"/query\", async (req: Request, res: Response) => {\n  const body = req.body as QueryRequest;\n\n  if (!body.message || !body.system_prompt) {\n    res.status(400).json({ error: \"message and system_prompt are required\" });\n    return;\n  }\n\n  // Set up SSE headers\n  res.setHeader(\"Content-Type\", \"text/event-stream\");\n  res.setHeader(\"Cache-Control\", \"no-cache\");\n  res.setHeader(\"Connection\", \"keep-alive\");\n  res.setHeader(\"X-Accel-Buffering\", \"no\");\n  res.flushHeaders();\n\n  const options: any = {\n    model: body.model || process.env.ANTHROPIC_MODEL || \"claude-opus-4-6\",\n    system_prompt: body.system_prompt,\n    allowed_tools: [\"mcp__duckdb__execute_sql\"],\n    permission_mode: \"bypassPermissions\",\n    max_turns: 20,\n    include_partial_messages: true,\n    ...(body.session_id ? { resume: body.session_id } : {}),\n  };\n\n  // Configure MCP server if URL is provided\n  if (body.mcp_server_url) {\n    options.mcp_servers = {\n      duckdb: {\n        type: \"sse\",\n        url: body.mcp_server_url,\n      },\n    };\n  }\n\n  const client = new ClaudeSDKClient(options);\n  activeClient = client;\n\n  try {\n    await client.connect();\n    await client.query(body.message, { session_id: body.session_id || \"default\" });\n\n    for await (const msg of client.receiveResponse()) {\n      // Forward all messages as JSON SSE events\n      // The host backend will parse and re-emit these\n      const data = JSON.stringify(msg);\n      res.write(`data: ${data}\\n\\n`);\n    }\n  } catch (err: any) {\n    const errorData = JSON.stringify({ type: \"error\", message: err.message || String(err) });\n    res.write(`event: error\\ndata: ${errorData}\\n\\n`);\n  } finally {\n    activeClient = null;\n    try {\n      await client.disconnect();\n    } catch {\n      // ignore disconnect errors\n    }\n    res.end();\n  }\n});\n\napp.post(\"/stop\", async (_req: Request, res: Response) => {\n  if (activeClient) {\n    try {\n      await activeClient.disconnect();\n    } catch {\n      // ignore\n    }\n    activeClient = null;\n    res.json({ status: \"stopped\" });\n  } else {\n    res.json({ status: \"no_active_session\" });\n  }\n});\n\napp.listen(PORT, \"0.0.0.0\", () => {\n  console.log(`Sidecar agent server listening on port ${PORT}`);\n});\n```\n\n> **Note:** The exact Claude Agent SDK TypeScript API may differ from the Python SDK. The implementer should check the `claude-agent-sdk` npm package documentation and adjust the import paths, class names, and method signatures accordingly. The core pattern (connect → query → receive stream → disconnect) should be the same. If the npm package doesn't exist or has a different API, use `@anthropic-ai/claude-code` CLI subprocess spawning with `--output-format stream-json` instead.\n\n**Step 2: Verify TypeScript compiles**\n\nRun: `cd sidecar && npx tsc --noEmit`\nExpected: No errors (or minimal fixable errors from SDK type mismatches).\n\n**Step 3: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add sidecar/src/server.ts\ngit commit -m \"feat(sidecar): implement HTTP server wrapping Claude Agent SDK\"\n```\n\n---\n\n### Task 3: Sidecar Dockerfile\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `sidecar/Dockerfile`\n- Create: `sidecar/.dockerignore`\n\n**Step 1: Create `sidecar/.dockerignore`**\n\n```\nnode_modules\ndist\n*.md\n```\n\n**Step 2: Create `sidecar/Dockerfile`**\n\n```dockerfile\nFROM python:3.12-slim AS base\n\n# Install Node.js 20\nRUN apt-get update && \\\n    apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends curl ca-certificates && \\\n    curl -fsSL https://deb.nodesource.com/setup_20.x | bash - && \\\n    apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends nodejs && \\\n    apt-get clean && \\\n    rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*\n\n# Install Claude CLI globally\nRUN npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code\n\n# Build the sidecar server\nWORKDIR /app\nCOPY package.json package-lock.json ./\nRUN npm ci --omit=dev\nCOPY tsconfig.json ./\nCOPY src/ ./src/\nRUN npx tsc\n\n# Create non-root user\nRUN useradd --create-home --shell /bin/bash appuser\n\nUSER appuser\nEXPOSE 3000\n\nCMD [\"node\", \"dist/server.js\"]\n```\n\n**Step 3: Build the image**\n\nRun: `cd sidecar && docker build -t duckdb-agent-sidecar:latest .`\nExpected: Image builds successfully.\n\n**Step 4: Test the image starts**\n\nRun: `docker run --rm -d --name sidecar-test -p 3001:3000 duckdb-agent-sidecar:latest && -v`\nExpected: FAIL — `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'app.container_manager'`\n\n**Step 3: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add backend/tests/test_container_manager.py\ngit commit -m \"test: add unit tests for ContainerManager (red phase)\"\n```\n\n---\n\n### Task 6: Container manager — implementation\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `backend/app/container_manager.py`\n- Modify: `backend/pyproject.toml:16` (add `docker` dependency)\n\n**Step 1: Add `docker` dependency to `backend/pyproject.toml`**\n\nIn the `[tool.poetry.dependencies]` section, add:\n\n```\ndocker = \"^7.0.0\"\n```\n\n**Step 2: Install the dependency**\n\nRun: `cd /Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend && poetry add docker`\n\n**Step 3: Write `backend/app/container_manager.py`**\n\n```python\nimport logging\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone\n\nimport docker\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass ContainerConfig:\n    image: str = \"duckdb-agent-sidecar:latest\"\n    runtime: str = \"runsc\"\n    memory_limit: str = \"256m\"\n    cpu_limit: float = 0.5\n    max_lifetime_seconds: int = 600\n    network: str = \"agent-sandbox\"\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass ContainerInfo:\n    container_id: str\n    session_id: str\n    ip_address: str\n    port: int = 3000\n    created_at: datetime = field(default_factory=lambda: datetime.now(timezone.utc))\n    _container: object = field(default=None, repr=False)\n\n    @property\n    def url(self) -> str:\n        return f\"http://{self.ip_address}:{self.port}\"\n\n\nclass ContainerManager:\n    \"\"\"Manages per-session gVisor-sandboxed sidecar containers.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, config: ContainerConfig | None = None):\n        self._config = config or ContainerConfig()\n        self._client = docker.from_env()\n        self._containers: dict[str, ContainerInfo] = {}\n\n    def create(self, session_id: str, env: dict[str, str]) -> ContainerInfo:\n        \"\"\"Spin up a new sidecar container for a session.\"\"\"\n        if session_id in self._containers:\n            return self._containers[session_id]\n\n        container = self._client.containers.run(\n            image=self._config.image,\n            detach=True,\n            runtime=self._config.runtime,\n            mem_limit=self._config.memory_limit,\n            nano_cpus=int(self._config.cpu_limit * 1e9),\n            read_only=True,\n            cap_drop=[\"ALL\"],\n            security_opt=[\"no-new-privileges\"],\n            tmpfs={\"/tmp\": \"size=50m\"},\n            network=self._config.network,\n            environment=env,\n            labels={\n                \"app\": \"duckdb-agent-sidecar\",\n                \"session_id\": session_id,\n            },\n            auto_remove=False,  # We remove manually to handle errors\n        )\n\n        # Reload to get network info\n        container.reload()\n        networks = container.attrs.get(\"NetworkSettings\", {}).get(\"Networks\", {})\n        network_info = networks.get(self._config.network, {})\n        ip_address = network_info.get(\"IPAddress\", \"127.0.0.1\")\n\n        info = ContainerInfo(\n            container_id=container.id,\n            session_id=session_id,\n            ip_address=ip_address,\n            _container=container,\n        )\n        self._containers[session_id] = info\n        logger.info(\n            \"Created sidecar container %s for session %s at %s\",\n            container.id[:12],\n            session_id,\n            info.url,\n        )\n        return info\n\n    def get(self, session_id: str) -> ContainerInfo | None:\n        \"\"\"Get container info for a session.\"\"\"\n        return self._containers.get(session_id)\n\n    def stop(self, session_id: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Stop and remove the container for a session.\"\"\"\n        info = self._containers.pop(session_id, None)\n        if info is None:\n            return\n\n        container = info._container\n        try:\n            container.stop(timeout=5)\n        except Exception as e:\n            logger.warning(\"Failed to stop container %s: %s\", info.container_id[:12], e)\n        try:\n            container.remove(force=True)\n        except Exception as e:\n            logger.warning(\"Failed to remove container %s: %s\", info.container_id[:12], e)\n\n        logger.info(\"Stopped sidecar container %s for session %s\", info.container_id[:12], session_id)\n\n    def cleanup_expired(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Remove containers that have exceeded max lifetime.\"\"\"\n        cutoff = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(seconds=self._config.max_lifetime_seconds)\n        expired = [\n            sid for sid, info in self._containers.items()\n            if info.created_at < cutoff\n        ]\n        for sid in expired:\n            logger.info(\"Container for session %s exceeded max lifetime, removing\", sid)\n            self.stop(sid)\n        return len(expired)\n\n    def shutdown_all(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Stop and remove all active containers. Called on backend shutdown.\"\"\"\n        session_ids = list(self._containers.keys())\n        for sid in session_ids:\n            self.stop(sid)\n        logger.info(\"Shut down %d sidecar containers\", len(session_ids))\n```\n\n**Step 4: Run tests to verify they pass**\n\nRun: `cd /Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend && poetry run pytest tests/test_container_manager.py -v`\nExpected: All tests PASS.\n\n**Step 5: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add backend/app/container_manager.py backend/pyproject.toml backend/poetry.lock\ngit commit -m \"feat: implement ContainerManager for Docker container lifecycle\"\n```\n\n---\n\n### Task 7: Integrate container manager into agent.py\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `backend/app/agent.py:106-302`\n\n**Step 1: Write a test for the container integration path**\n\nCreate `backend/tests/test_agent_container.py`:\n\n```python\nfrom unittest.mock import patch, MagicMock\n\nfrom app.config import CONTAINER_ENABLED\n\n\ndef test_container_enabled_reads_from_config():\n    \"\"\"Verify the config flag is available and defaults to False.\"\"\"\n    # This just validates the import — integration is tested manually\n    assert CONTAINER_ENABLED is False or CONTAINER_ENABLED is True\n```\n\n**Step 2: Modify `backend/app/agent.py`**\n\nAdd the container-based streaming path. The key change is in `stream_chat()`: when `CONTAINER_ENABLED` is `True`, instead of creating a local `ClaudeSDKClient`, the function:\n\n1. Creates or reuses a container via `ContainerManager`\n2. Sends a POST request to the sidecar's `/query` endpoint\n3. Streams the SSE response back\n\nAt the top of `agent.py`, add imports:\n\n```python\nfrom app.config import ANTHROPIC_MODEL, PROXY_BASE_URL, CONTAINER_ENABLED\n```\n\nAdd a new function `_stream_chat_container()` after `_extract_tool_result_text()` (before `stream_chat()`):\n\n```python\nasync def _stream_chat_container(\n    message: str,\n    session_id: str | None,\n    db: Database,\n    conversation_history: list[dict] | None,\n    container_manager,\n) -> AsyncIterator[str]:\n    \"\"\"Stream chat via containerized sidecar instead of local subprocess.\"\"\"\n    import httpx\n\n    query_message = _build_message_with_history(message, conversation_history)\n    system_prompt = build_system_prompt(db)\n\n    session_token=[REDACTED].create_token()\n\n    env = {\n        \"ANTHROPIC_API_KEY\": session_token,\n        \"ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL\": f\"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/anthropic\",\n        \"LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY\": \"\",\n        \"LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY\": \"\",\n    }\n\n    try:\n        # Create or reuse container for this session\n        container_session = session_id or \"default\"\n        info = container_manager.create(container_session, env)\n\n        # Wait for container to be ready\n        import asyncio\n        for attempt in range(10):\n            try:\n                async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(5.0)) as check_client:\n                    resp = await check_client.get(f\"{info.url}/health\")\n                    if resp.status_code == 200:\n                        break\n            except Exception:\n                pass\n            await asyncio.sleep(1)\n        else:\n            raise RuntimeError(f\"Sidecar container failed health check after 10 attempts\")\n\n        # Send query to sidecar\n        payload = {\n            \"message\": query_message,\n            \"session_id\": session_id,\n            \"system_prompt\": system_prompt,\n            \"model\": ANTHROPIC_MODEL,\n            \"mcp_server_url\": f\"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/mcp\",\n        }\n\n        async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0)) as client:\n            async with client.stream(\"POST\", f\"{info.url}/query\", json=payload) as response:\n                async for line in response.aiter_lines():\n                    if line.startswith(\"data: \"):\n                        # Forward SSE data lines directly\n                        yield f\"{line}\\n\\n\"\n                    elif line.startswith(\"event: \"):\n                        yield f\"{line}\\n\"\n\n    except Exception as e:\n        logger.error(\"Container agent error: %s\", str(e))\n        yield f\"event: error\\ndata: {json.dumps({'message': str(e)})}\\n\\n\"\n    finally:\n        proxy_token_store.revoke_token(session_token)\n```\n\nThen modify `stream_chat()` to dispatch between the two paths. At the beginning of `stream_chat()`, add:\n\n```python\n    if CONTAINER_ENABLED:\n        # Import here to avoid import error when docker is not installed\n        from app.container_manager import container_manager\n        async for event in _stream_chat_container(\n            message, session_id, db, conversation_history, container_manager\n        ):\n            yield event\n        return\n```\n\n**Step 3: Run existing tests to verify no regression**\n\nRun: `cd /Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend && poetry run pytest tests/ -v`\nExpected: All existing tests PASS      1→# Containerized Claude Code Runtime Implementation Plan
     2→
     3→> **For Claude:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task.
     4→
     5→**Goal:** Run each Claude Code session inside a gVisor-sandboxed Docker container ("sidecar") for code execution sandboxing, multi-tenant isolation, and defense in depth.
     6→
     7→**Architecture:** A lightweight TypeScript HTTP server inside a Docker container wraps the Claude Agent SDK. The host FastAPI backend manages container lifecycle via Docker SDK for Python. DuckDB stays on the host; the sidecar reaches it via MCP over the host network. A feature flag (`CONTAINER_ENABLED`) allows fallback to the existing subprocess model.
     8→
     9→**Tech Stack:** TypeScript (sidecar server), Python (container manager), Docker SDK for Python (`docker` package), gVisor (`runsc` runtime), Express.js (sidecar HTTP framework)

   100→### Task 2: Sidecar HTTP server implementation
   101→

   104→
   105→**Step 1: Write `sidecar/src/server.ts`**
   106→
   107→This is the thin HTTP wrapper around the Claude Agent SDK. It exposes three endpoints:
   108→
   109→- `GET /health` — readiness probe
   110→- `POST /query` — accept a query, stream SSE response
   111→- `POST /stop` — gracefully stop current session
   112→
   113→```typescript
   114→import express, { Request, Response } from "express";
   115→import { ClaudeSDKClient, ClaudeAgentOptions } from "claude-agent-sdk";
   116→import type { QueryRequest, HealthResponse } from "./types.js";
   117→
   118→const app = express();
   119→app.use(express.json());
   120→
   121→const PORT = parseInt(process.env.PORT || "3000", 10);
   122→
   123→// Track active client for /stop endpoint
   124→let activeClient: ClaudeSDKClient | null = null;
   125→
   126→app.get("/health", (_req: Request, res: Response) => {
   127→  const response: HealthResponse = { status: "ok" };
   128→  res.json(response);
   129→});
   130→
   131→app.post("/query", async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
   132→  const body = req.body as QueryRequest;
   133→
   134→  if (!body.message || !body.system_prompt) {
   135→    res.status(400).json({ error: "message and system_prompt are required" });
   136→    return;
   137→  }
   138→
   139→  // Set up SSE headers
   140→  res.setHeader("Content-Type", "text/event-stream");
   141→  res.setHeader("Cache-Control", "no-cache");
   142→  res.setHeader("Connection", "keep-alive");
   143→  res.setHeader("X-Accel-Buffering", "no");
   144→  res.flushHeaders();
   145→
   146→  const options: any = {
   147→    model: body.model || process.env.ANTHROPIC_MODEL || "claude-opus-4-6",
   148→    system_prompt: body.system_prompt,
   149→    allowed_tools: ["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"],
   150→    permission_mode: "bypassPermissions",
   151→    max_turns: 20,
   152→    include_partial_messages: true,
   153→    ...(body.session_id ? { resume: body.session_id } : {}),
   154→  };
   155→
   156→  // Configure MCP server if URL is provided
   157→  if (body.mcp_server_url) {
   158→    options.mcp_servers = {
   159→      duckdb: {
   160→        type: "sse",
   161→        url: body.mcp_server_url,
   162→      },
   163→    };
   164→  }
   165→
   166→  const client = new ClaudeSDKClient(options);
   167→  activeClient = client;
   168→
   169→  try {
   170→    await client.connect();
   171→    await client.query(body.message, { session_id: body.session_id || "default" });
   172→
   173→    for await (const msg of client.receiveResponse()) {
   174→      // Forward all messages as JSON SSE events
   175→      // The host backend will parse and re-emit these
   176→      const data = JSON.stringify(msg);
   177→      res.write(`data: ${data}\n\n`);
   178→    }
   179→  } catch (err: any) {
   180→    const errorData = JSON.stringify({ type: "error", message: err.message || String(err) });
   181→    res.write(`event: error\ndata: ${errorData}\n\n`);
   182→  } finally {
   183→    activeClient = null;
   184→    try {
   185→      await client.disconnect();
   186→    } catch {
   187→      // ignore disconnect errors
   188→    }
   189→    res.end();
   190→  }
   191→});
   192→
   193→app.post("/stop", async (_req: Request, res: Response) => {
   194→  if (activeClient) {
   195→    try {
   196→      await activeClient.disconnect();
   197→    } catch {
   198→      // ignore
   199→    }
   200→    activeClient = null;
   201→    res.json({ status: "stopped" });
   202→  } else {
   203→    res.json({ status: "no_active_session" });
   204→  }
   205→});
   206→
   207→app.listen(PORT, "0.0.0.0", () => {
   208→  console.log(`Sidecar agent server listening on port ${PORT}`);
   209→});
   210→```
   211→
   212→> **Note:** The exact Claude Agent SDK TypeScript API may differ from the Python SDK. The implementer should check the `claude-agent-sdk` npm package documentation and adjust the import paths, class names, and method signatures accordingly. The core pattern (connect → query → receive stream → disconnect) should be the same. If the npm package doesn't exist or has a different API, use `@anthropic-ai/claude-code` CLI subprocess spawning with `--output-format stream-json` instead.
   213→

   215→
   216→Run: `cd sidecar && npx tsc --noEmit`
   217→Expected: No errors (or minimal fixable errors from SDK type mismatches).

   220→

   223→git commit -m "feat(sidecar): implement HTTP server wrapping Claude Agent SDK"

   242→**Step 2: Create `sidecar/Dockerfile`**
   243→
   244→```dockerfile
   245→FROM python:3.12-slim AS base
   246→
   247→# Install Node.js 20
   248→RUN apt-get update && \
   249→    apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends curl ca-certificates && \
   250→    curl -fsSL https://deb.nodesource.com/setup_20.x | bash - && \
   251→    apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends nodejs && \
   252→    apt-get clean && \
   253→    rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*
   254→
   255→# Install Claude CLI globally
   256→RUN npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code
   257→
   258→# Build the sidecar server
   259→WORKDIR /app
   260→COPY package.json package-lock.json ./
   261→RUN npm ci --omit=dev
   262→COPY tsconfig.json ./
   263→COPY src/ ./src/
   264→RUN npx tsc
   265→
   266→# Create non-root user
   267→RUN useradd --create-home --shell /bin/bash appuser
   268→
   269→USER appuser
   270→EXPOSE 3000
   271→
   272→CMD ["node", "dist/server.js"]
   273→```

   518→### Task 6: Container manager — implementation
   519→
   520→**Files:**
   521→- Create: `backend/app/container_manager.py`
   522→- Modify: `backend/pyproject.toml:16` (add `docker` dependency)
   523→
   524→**Step 1: Add `docker` dependency to `backend/pyproject.toml`**
   525→
   526→In the `[tool.poetry.dependencies]` section, add:
   527→
   528→```
   529→docker = "^7.0.0"
   530→```
   531→
   532→**Step 2: Install the dependency**
   533→
   534→Run: `cd /Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/backend && poetry add docker`
   535→
   536→**Step 3: Write `backend/app/container_manager.py`**
   537→
   538→```python
   539→import logging
   540→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
   541→from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
   542→
   543→import docker
   544→
   545→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
   546→
   547→
   548→@dataclass
   549→class ContainerConfig:
   550→    image: str = "duckdb-agent-sidecar:latest"
   551→    runtime: str = "runsc"
   552→    memory_limit: str = "256m"
   553→    cpu_limit: float = 0.5
   554→    max_lifetime_seconds: int = 600
   555→    network: str = "agent-sandbox"
   556→
   557→
   558→@dataclass
   559→class ContainerInfo:
   560→    container_id: str
   561→    session_id: str
   562→    ip_address: str
   563→    port: int = 3000
   564→    created_at: datetime = field(default_factory=lambda: datetime.now(timezone.utc))
   565→    _container: object = field(default=None, repr=False)
   566→
   567→    @property
   568→    def url(self) -> str:
   569→        return f"http://{self.ip_address}:{self.port}"
   570→
   571→
   572→class ContainerManager:
   573→    """Manages per-session gVisor-sandboxed sidecar containers."""
   574→
   575→    def __init__(self, config: ContainerConfig | None = None):
   576→        self._config = config or ContainerConfig()
   577→        self._client = docker.from_env()
   578→        self._containers: dict[str, ContainerInfo] = {}
   579→
   580→    def create(self, session_id: str, env: dict[str, str]) -> ContainerInfo:
   581→        """Spin up a new sidecar container for a session."""
   582→        if session_id in self._containers:
   583→            return self._containers[session_id]
   584→
   585→        container = self._client.containers.run(
   586→            image=self._config.image,
   587→            detach=True,
   588→            runtime=self._config.runtime,
   589→            mem_limit=self._config.memory_limit,
   590→            nano_cpus=int(self._config.cpu_limit * 1e9),
   591→            read_only=True,
   592→            cap_drop=["ALL"],
   593→            security_opt=["no-new-privileges"],
   594→            tmpfs={"/tmp": "size=50m"},
   595→            network=self._config.network,
   596→            environment=env,
   597→            labels={
   598→                "app": "duckdb-agent-sidecar",
   599→                "session_id": session_id,
   600→            },
   601→            auto_remove=False,  # We remove manually to handle errors
   602→        )
   603→
   604→        # Reload to get network info
   605→        container.reload()
   606→        networks = container.attrs.get("NetworkSettings", {}).get("Networks", {})
   607→        network_info = networks.get(self._config.network, {})
   608→        ip_address = network_info.get("IPAddress", "127.0.0.1")
   609→
   610→        info = ContainerInfo(
   611→            container_id=container.id,
   612→            session_id=session_id,
   613→            ip_address=ip_address,
   614→            _container=container,
   615→        )
   616→        self._containers[session_id] = info
   617→        logger.info(
   618→            "Created sidecar container %s for session %s at %s",
   619→            container.id[:12],
   620→            session_id,
   621→            info.url,
   622→        )
   623→        return info
   624→
   625→    def get(self, session_id: str) -> ContainerInfo | None:
   626→        """Get container info for a session."""
   627→        return self._containers.get(session_id)
   628→
   629→    def stop(self, session_id: str) -> None:
   630→        """Stop and remove the container for a session."""
   631→        info = self._containers.pop(session_id, None)
   632→        if info is None:
   633→            return
   634→
   635→        container = info._container
   636→        try:
   637→            container.stop(timeout=5)
   638→        except Exception as e:
   639→            logger.warning("Failed to stop container %s: %s", info.container_id[:12], e)
   640→        try:
   641→            container.remove(force=True)
   642→        except Exception as e:
   643→            logger.warning("Failed to remove container %s: %s", info.container_id[:12], e)
   644→
   645→        logger.info("Stopped sidecar container %s for session %s", info.container_id[:12], session_id)
   646→
   647→    def cleanup_expired(self) -> int:
   648→        """Remove containers that have exceeded max lifetime."""
   649→        cutoff = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(seconds=self._config.max_lifetime_seconds)
   650→        expired = [
   651→            sid for sid, info in self._containers.items()
   652→            if info.created_at < cutoff
   653→        ]
   654→        for sid in expired:
   655→            logger.info("Container for session %s exceeded max lifetime, removing", sid)
   656→            self.stop(sid)
   657→        return len(expired)
   658→
   659→    def shutdown_all(self) -> None:
   660→        """Stop and remove all active containers. Called on backend shutdown."""
   661→        session_ids = list(self._containers.keys())
   662→        for sid in session_ids:
   663→            self.stop(sid)
   664→        logger.info("Shut down %d sidecar containers", len(session_ids))
   665→```
   666→

   670→Expected: All tests PASS.

   676→git commit -m "feat: implement ContainerManager for Docker container lifecycle"

   681→### Task 7: Integrate container manager into agent.py
   682→
   683→**Files:**
   684→- Modify: `backend/app/agent.py:106-302`
   685→
   686→**Step 1: Write a test for the container integration path**
   687→
   688→Create `backend/tests/test_agent_container.py`:
   689→
   690→```python
   691→from unittest.mock import patch, MagicMock
   692→
   693→from app.config import CONTAINER_ENABLED
   694→
   695→
   696→def test_container_enabled_reads_from_config():
   697→    """Verify the config flag is available and defaults to False."""

   701→
   702→**Step 2: Modify `backend/app/agent.py`**
   703→
   704→Add the container-based streaming path. The key change is in `stream_chat()`: when `CONTAINER_ENABLED` is `True`, instead of creating a local `ClaudeSDKClient`, the function:
   705→
   706→1. Creates or reuses a container via `ContainerManager`
   707→2. Sends a POST request to the sidecar's `/query` endpoint
   708→3. Streams the SSE response back
   709→
   710→At the top of `agent.py`, add imports:
   711→
   712→```python
   713→from app.config import ANTHROPIC_MODEL, PROXY_BASE_URL, CONTAINER_ENABLED
   714→```
   715→
   716→Add a new function `_stream_chat_container()` after `_extract_tool_result_text()` (before `stream_chat()`):
   717→
   718→```python
   719→async def _stream_chat_container(
   720→    message: str,
   721→    session_id: str | None,
   722→    db: Database,
   723→    conversation_history: list[dict] | None,
   724→    container_manager,
   725→) -> AsyncIterator[str]:
   726→    """Stream chat via containerized sidecar instead of local subprocess."""
   727→    import httpx
   728→
   729→    query_message = _build_message_with_history(message, conversation_history)
   730→    system_prompt = build_system_prompt(db)
   731→
   732→    session_token=[REDACTED].create_token()
   733→
   734→    env = {
   735→        "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY": session_token,
   736→        "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/anthropic",
   737→        "LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY": "",
   738→        "LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY": "",
   739→    }
   740→
   741→    try:
   742→        # Create or reuse container for this session
   743→        container_session = session_id or "default"
   744→        info = container_manager.create(container_session, env)
   745→
   746→        # Wait for container to be ready
   747→        import asyncio
   748→        for attempt in range(10):
   749→            try:
   750→                async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(5.0)) as check_client:
   751→                    resp = await check_client.get(f"{info.url}/health")
   752→                    if resp.status_code == 200:
   753→                        break
   754→            except Exception:
   755→                pass
   756→            await asyncio.sleep(1)
   757→        else:
   758→            raise RuntimeError(f"Sidecar container failed health check after 10 attempts")
   759→
   760→        # Send query to sidecar
   761→        payload = {
   762→            "message": query_message,
   763→            "session_id": session_id,
   764→            "system_prompt": system_prompt,
   765→            "model": ANTHROPIC_MODEL,
   766→            "mcp_server_url": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/mcp",
   767→        }
   768→
   769→        async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0)) as client:
   770→            async with client.stream("POST", f"{info.url}/query", json=payload) as response:
   771→                async for line in response.aiter_lines():
   772→                    if line.startswith("data: "):
   773→                        # Forward SSE data lines directly
   774→                        yield f"{line}\n\n"
   775→                    elif line.startswith("event: "):
   776→                        yield f"{line}\n"
   777→
   778→    except Exception as e:
   779→        logger.error("Container agent error: %s", str(e))
   780→        yield f"event: error\ndata: {json.dumps({'message': str(e)})}\n\n"
   781→    finally:
   782→        proxy_token_store.revoke_token(session_token)
   783→```
   784→
   785→Then modify `stream_chat()` to dispatch between the two paths. At the beginning of `stream_chat()`, add:
   786→
   787→```python
   788→    if CONTAINER_ENABLED:
   789→        # Import here to avoid import error when docker is not installed
   790→        from app.container_manager import container_manager
   791→        async for event in _stream_chat_container(
   792→            message, session_id, db, conversation_history, container_manager
   793→        ):
   794→            yield event
   795→        return
   796→```

   812→### Task 8: Integrate container manager into main.py lifecycle
   813→
   814→**Files:**
   815→- Modify: `backend/app/main.py:19-34`
   816→
   817→**Step 1: Update `_cleanup_loop` and `lifespan` in `main.py`**
   818→
   819→Add container cleanup to the background loop and shutdown handler:
   820→
   821→After the existing imports at the top of `main.py`, add:

   826→
   827→Modify `_cleanup_loop()` to add container cleanup:
   828→
   829→```python
   830→async def _cleanup_loop():
   831→    while True:
   832→        await asyncio.sleep(60)
   833→        removed = session_manager.cleanup_stale(ttl_seconds=300)
   834→        if removed:
   835→            logger.info("Background cleanup: removed %d stale sessions", removed)
   836→        proxy_removed = proxy_module.proxy_token_store.cleanup_expired()
   837→        if proxy_removed:
   838→            logger.info("Background cleanup: removed %d expired proxy tokens", proxy_removed)
   839→        if CONTAINER_ENABLED:
   840→            from app.container_manager import container_manager
   841→            container_removed = container_manager.cleanup_expired()
   842→            if container_removed:
   843→                logger.info("Background cleanup: removed %d expired containers", container_removed)
   844→```
   845→
   846→Modify `lifespan()` to add container shutdown:
   847→
   848→```python
   849→@asynccontextmanager
   850→async def lifespan(app):
   851→    task = asyncio.create_task(_cleanup_loop())
   852→    yield
   853→    task.cancel()
   854→    if CONTAINER_ENABLED:
   855→        from app.container_manager import container_manager
   856→        container_manager.shutdown_all()
   857→```

   918→### Task 10: Docker network setup script
   919→
   920→**Files:**
   921→- Create: `sidecar/setup-network.sh`
   922→
   923→**Step 1: Create `sidecar/setup-network.sh`**
   924→
   925→This script creates the Docker network with internal network restrictions.
   926→
   927→```bash
   928→#!/usr/bin/env bash
   929→set -euo pipefail
   930→
   931→NETWORK_NAME="${CONTAINER_NETWORK:-agent-sandbox}"
   932→
   933→# Create the bridge network if it doesn't exist
   934→if ! docker network inspect "$NETWORK_NAME" &>/dev/null; then
   935→    echo "Creating Docker network: $NETWORK_NAME"
   936→    docker network create \
   937→        --driver bridge \
   938→        --internal=false \
   939→        "$NETWORK_NAME"
   940→    echo "Network $NETWORK_NAME created."
   941→else
   942→    echo "Network $NETWORK_NAME already exists."
   943→fi
   944→
   945→echo ""
   946→echo "To block internal network access from containers, add iptables rules:"
   947→echo "  iptables -I DOCKER-USER -s 172.18.0.0/16 -d 10.0.0.0/8 -j DROP"
   948→echo "  iptables -I DOCKER-USER -s 172.18.0.0/16 -d 172.16.0.0/12 -j DROP"
   949→echo "  iptables -I DOCKER-USER -s 172.18.0.0/16 -d 192.168.0.0/16 -j DROP"
   950→echo "  iptables -I DOCKER-USER -s 172.18.0.0/16 -d 169.254.0.0/16 -j DROP"
   951→echo ""
   952→echo "Note: Allow the host proxy address explicitly before applying these rules."
   953→```
   954→
   955→**Step 2: Make it executable**
   956→
   957→Run: `chmod +x sidecar/setup-network.sh`

    50→    - Did I miss any requirements?
    51→    - Are there edge cases I didn't handle?
    52→

    55→    - Are names clear and accurate (match what things do, not how they work)?
    56→    - Is the code clean and maintainable?
    57→
    58→    **Discipline:**
    59→    - Did I avoid overbuilding (YAGNI)?

    66→    - Are tests comprehensive?
    67→
    68→    If you find issues during self-review, fix them now before reporting.

The sidecar will use `@anthropic-ai/claude-code` CLI which is installed globally in the Docker image. Remove it from dependencies.\n\n**Step 2: Create `sidecar/tsconfig.json`**\n\n```json\n{\n  \"compilerOptions\": {\n    \"target\": \"ES2022\",\n    \"module\": \"NodeNext\",\n    \"moduleResolution\": \"NodeNext\",\n    \"outDir\": \"dist\",\n    \"rootDir\": \"src\",\n    \"strict\": true,\n    \"esModuleInterop\": true,\n    \"skipLibCheck\": true,\n    \"declaration\": true\n  },\n  \"include\": [\"src\"]\n}\n```\n\n**Step 3: Create `sidecar/src/types.ts`**\n\n```typescript\nexport interface QueryRequest {\n  message: string;\n  session_id?: string;\n  system_prompt: string;\n  model?: string;\n  mcp_server_url?: string;\n}\n\nexport interface HealthResponse {\n  status: \"ok\" | \"error\";\n  message?: string;\n}\n```\n\n**Step 4: Install dependencies**\n\nRun: `cd sidecar && npm install`\nExpected: `node_modules/` created, `package-lock.json` generated.\n\n**Step 5: Verify TypeScript compiles**\n\nRun: `cd sidecar && npx tsc --noEmit`\nExpected: No errors.\n\n**Step 6: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add sidecar/package.json sidecar/package-lock.json sidecar/tsconfig.json sidecar/src/types.ts\ngit commit -m \"feat(sidecar): scaffold TypeScript project for agent sidecar\"\n```\n\n## Context\n\nThis is the first task in building a containerized Claude Code runtime. {

  "prompt": "You are implementing Task 2: Sidecar HTTP server implementation\n\n## Task Description\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `sidecar/src/server.ts`\n\nCreate an Express HTTP server that wraps the `@anthropic-ai/claude-code` CLI. It exposes three endpoints:\n\n- `GET /health` — readiness probe, returns `{\"status\":\"ok\"}`\n- `POST /query` — accept a QueryRequest body, spawn the Claude CLI as a subprocess with `--output-format stream-json`, stream the response back as SSE events\n- `POST /stop` — gracefully stop the current CLI subprocess\n\n**IMPORTANT:** There is NO npm package called `claude-agent-sdk` for TypeScript. Instead, you must use `@anthropic-ai/claude-code` CLI binary (installed globally in the Docker container). Spawn it as a child process using Node.js `child_process.spawn`.\n\nThe Claude CLI can be invoked like:\n```\nclaude --output-format stream-json --model <model> --system-prompt <prompt> --allowedTools mcp__duckdb__execute_sql --permission-mode bypassPermissions --max-turns 20 -p \"<message>\"\n```\n\nIt outputs newline-delimited JSON to stdout. Each JSON object has a `type` field. Forward these as SSE data events.\n\nFor MCP server configuration, use the `--mcp-config` flag with a temporary JSON file.\n\nEnvironment variables `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` will be set by the container's env — the CLI reads them automatically.\n\nHere's a reference implementation approach:\n\n```typescript\nimport express, { Request, Response } from \"express\";\nimport { spawn, ChildProcess } from \"child_process\";\nimport { createWriteStream, mkdtempSync, writeFileSync, unlinkSync } from \"fs\";\nimport { tmpdir } from \"os\";\nimport { join } from \"path\";\nimport type { QueryRequest, HealthResponse } from \"./types.js\";\n\nconst app = express();\napp.use(express.json({ limit: \"10mb\" }));\n\nconst PORT = parseInt(process.env.PORT || \"3000\", 10);\n\nlet activeProcess: ChildProcess | null = null;\n\napp.get(\"/health\", (_req: Request, res: Response) => {\n  const response: HealthResponse = { status: \"ok\" };\n  res.json(response);\n});\n\napp.post(\"/query\", async (req: Request, res: Response) => {\n  const body = req.body as QueryRequest;\n\n  if (!body.message || !body.system_prompt) {\n    res.status(400).json({ error: \"message and system_prompt are required\" });\n    return;\n  }\n\n  // Set up SSE headers\n  res.setHeader(\"Content-Type\", \"text/event-stream\");\n  res.setHeader(\"Cache-Control\", \"no-cache\");\n  res.setHeader(\"Connection\", \"keep-alive\");\n  res.setHeader(\"X-Accel-Buffering\", \"no\");\n  res.flushHeaders();\n\n  // Write MCP config to temp file if mcp_server_url provided\n  let mcpConfigPath: string | null = null;\n  if (body.mcp_server_url) {\n    const tmpDir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), \"sidecar-\"));\n    mcpConfigPath = join(tmpDir, \"mcp.json\");\n    writeFileSync(mcpConfigPath, JSON.stringify({\n      mcpServers: {\n        duckdb: {\n          type: \"sse\",\n          url: body.mcp_server_url,\n        },\n      },\n    }));\n  }\n\n  const args = [\n    \"--output-format\", \"stream-json\",\n    \"--model\", body.model || process.env.ANTHROPIC_MODEL || \"claude-opus-4-6\",\n    \"--system-prompt\", body.system_prompt,\n    \"--allowedTools\", \"mcp__duckdb__execute_sql\",\n    \"--permission-mode\", \"bypassPermissions\",\n    \"--max-turns\", \"20\",\n    ...(body.session_id ? [\"--resume\", body.session_id] : []),\n    ...(mcpConfigPath ? [\"--mcp-config\", mcpConfigPath] : []),\n    \"-p\", body.message,\n  ];\n\n  const proc = spawn(\"claude\", args, {\n    env: process.env as Record<string, string>,\n    stdio: [\"pipe\", \"pipe\", \"pipe\"],\n  });\n\n  activeProcess = proc;\n\n  let buffer = \"\";\n\n  proc.stdout.on(\"data\", (chunk: Buffer) => {\n    buffer += chunk.toString();\n    const lines = buffer.split(\"\\n\");\n    buffer = lines.pop() || \"\";\n    for (const line of lines) {\n      if (line.trim()) {\n        res.write(`data: ${line}\\n\\n`);\n      }\n    }\n  });\n\n  proc.stderr.on(\"data\", (chunk: Buffer) => {\n    console.error(`[claude stderr] ${chunk.toString()}`);\n  });\n\n  proc.on(\"close\", (code) => {\n    // Flush remaining buffer\n    if (buffer.trim()) {\n      res.write(`data: ${buffer}\\n\\n`);\n    }\n    activeProcess = null;\n\n    // Clean up MCP config temp file\n    if (mcpConfigPath) {\n      try { unlinkSync(mcpConfigPath); } catch {}\n    }\n\n    if (code !== 0) {\n      res.write(`event: error\\ndata: ${JSON.stringify({ type: \"error\", message: `Claude CLI exited with code ${code}` })}\\n\\n`);\n    }\n    res.end();\n  });\n\n  proc.on(\"error\", (err) => {\n    activeProcess = null;\n    res.write(`event: error\\ndata: ${JSON.stringify({ type: \"error\", message: err.message })}\\n\\n`);\n    res.end();\n  });\n\n  // Handle client disconnect\n  req.on(\"close\", () => {\n    if (proc && !proc.killed) {\n      proc.kill(\"SIGTERM\");\n    }\n  });\n});\n\napp.post(\"/stop\", (_req: Request, res: Response) => {\n  if (activeProcess && !activeProcess.killed) {\n    activeProcess.kill(\"SIGTERM\");\n    activeProcess = null;\n    res.json({ status: \"stopped\" });\n  } else {\n    res.json({ status: \"no_active_session\" });\n  }\n});\n\napp.listen(PORT, \"0.0.0.0\", () => {\n  console.log(`Sidecar agent server listening on port ${PORT}`);\n});\n```\n\n**Step 2: Verify TypeScript compiles**\n\nRun: `cd /Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/sidecar && npx tsc --noEmit`\nExpected: No errors.\n\n**Step 3: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add sidecar/src/server.ts\ngit commit -m \"feat(sidecar): implement HTTP server wrapping Claude CLI subprocess\"\n```\n\n## Context\n\nThis is the HTTP server that runs inside the gVisor-sandboxed Docker container. It wraps the `@anthropic-ai/claude-code` CLI binary (not an npm package) as a child process. The host FastAPI backend will communicate with this server via HTTP to send queries and receive streaming responses.\n\nThe CLI is installed globally via `npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code` in the Dockerfile. ## Summary

I created the file `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/sidecar/src/server.ts` implementing the sidecar HTTP server. Here is what the server does:

### Endpoints

1. **`GET /health`** -- Returns `{"status":"ok"}` for readiness probes.

2. **`POST /query`** -- Accepts a `QueryRequest` JSON body with `message`, `system_prompt`, and optional `session_id`, `model`, and `mcp_server_url` fields. It:
   - Validates that `message` and `system_prompt` are present (returns 400 if not).
   - Sets up SSE response headers (`Content-Type: text/event-stream`, etc.).
   - If `mcp_server_url` is provided, writes a temporary MCP config JSON file for the `--mcp-config` flag.
   - Spawns the `claude` CLI binary as a child process with `--output-format stream-json`, `--permission-mode bypassPermissions`, `--max-turns 20`, and other relevant flags.
   - Streams stdout line-by-line as SSE `data:` events.
   - On process close, flushes any remaining buffered data, cleans up the temp MCP config file, and sends an error event if the exit code was non-zero.
   - Handles client disconnects by sending SIGTERM to the subprocess.

3. **`POST /stop`** -- Gracefully stops the active CLI subprocess by sending SIGTERM. Returns `{"status":"stopped"}` or `{"status":"no_active_session"}`.

### Key implementation details

- Uses `child_process.spawn` (not an npm SDK) to invoke the globally-installed `@anthropic-ai/claude-code` CLI binary.
- The CLI automatically reads `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` from the environment.
- TypeScript compiles cleanly with `npx tsc --noEmit` (zero errors).
- Committed as `14e697e feat(sidecar): implement HTTP server wrapping Claude CLI subprocess`.

dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone\n\nimport docker\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass ContainerConfig:\n    image: str = \"duckdb-agent-sidecar:latest\"\n    runtime: str = \"runsc\"\n    memory_limit: str = \"256m\"\n    cpu_limit: float = 0.5\n    max_lifetime_seconds: int = 600\n    network: str = \"agent-sandbox\"\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass ContainerInfo:\n    container_id: str\n    session_id: str\n    ip_address: str\n    port: int = 3000\n    created_at: datetime = field(default_factory=lambda: datetime.now(timezone.utc))\n    _container: object = field(default=None, repr=False)\n\n    @property\n    def url(self) -> str:\n        return f\"http://{self.ip_address}:{self.port}\"\n\n\nclass ContainerManager:\n    \"\"\"Manages per-session gVisor-sandboxed sidecar containers.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, config: ContainerConfig | None = None):\n        self._config = config or ContainerConfig()\n        self._client = docker.from_env()\n        self._containers: dict[str, ContainerInfo] = {}\n\n    def create(self, session_id: str, env: dict[str, str]) -> ContainerInfo:\n        \"\"\"Spin up a new sidecar container for a session.\"\"\"\n        if session_id in self._containers:\n            return self._containers[session_id]\n\n        container = self._client.containers.run(\n            image=self._config.image,\n            detach=True,\n            runtime=self._config.runtime,\n            mem_limit=self._config.memory_limit,\n            nano_cpus=int(self._config.cpu_limit * 1e9),\n            read_only=True,\n            cap_drop=[\"ALL\"],\n            security_opt=[\"no-new-privileges\"],\n            tmpfs={\"/tmp\": \"size=50m\"},\n            network=self._config.network,\n            environment=env,\n            labels={\n                \"app\": \"duckdb-agent-sidecar\",\n                \"session_id\": session_id,\n            },\n            auto_remove=False,\n        )\n\n        container.reload()\n        networks = container.attrs.get(\"NetworkSettings\", {}).get(\"Networks\", {})\n        network_info = networks.get(self._config.network, {})\n        ip_address = network_info.get(\"IPAddress\", \"127.0.0.1\")\n\n        info = ContainerInfo(\n            container_id=container.id,\n            session_id=session_id,\n            ip_address=ip_address,\n            _container=container,\n        )\n        self._containers[session_id] = info\n        logger.info(\n            \"Created sidecar container %s for session %s at %s\",\n            container.id[:12],\n            session_id,\n            info.url,\n        )\n        return info\n\n    def get(self, session_id: str) -> ContainerInfo | None:\n        \"\"\"Get container info for a session.\"\"\"\n        return self._containers.get(session_id)\n\n    def stop(self, session_id: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Stop and remove the container for a session.\"\"\"\n        info = self._containers.pop(session_id, None)\n        if info is None:\n            return\n\n        container = info._container\n        try:\n            container.stop(timeout=5)\n        except Exception as e:\n            logger.warning(\"Failed to stop container %s: %s\", info.container_id[:12], e)\n        try:\n            container.remove(force=True)\n        except Exception as e:\n            logger.warning(\"Failed to remove container %s: %s\", info.container_id[:12], e)\n\n        logger.info(\"Stopped sidecar container %s for session %s\", info.container_id[:12], session_id)\n\n    def cleanup_expired(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Remove containers that have exceeded max lifetime.\"\"\"\n        cutoff = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(seconds=self._config.max_lifetime_seconds)\n        expired = [\n            sid for sid, info in self._containers.items()\n            if info.created_at < cutoff\n        ]\n        for sid in expired:\n            logger.info(\"Container for session %s exceeded max lifetime, removing\", sid)\n            self.stop(sid)\n        return len(expired)\n\n    def shutdown_all(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Stop and remove all active containers. Called on backend shutdown.\"\"\"\n        session_ids = list(self._containers.keys())\n        for sid in session_ids:\n            self.stop(sid)\n        logger.info(\"Shut down %d sidecar containers\", len(session_ids))\n```\n\n**IMPORTANT:** Do NOT add a module-level singleton at the bottom of this file. ### Task 6: Container Manager Implementation (Green Phase)

- **`ContainerInfo`** - Dataclass holding container metadata (ID, session ID, IP, port, creation time, Docker container reference) with a `url` property.
- **`ContainerManager`** - Core class managing Docker container lifecycle:
  - `create(session_id, env)` - Spins up a gVisor-sandboxed container with security hardening (read-only FS, all capabilities dropped, no-new-privileges, tmpfs for /tmp).
  - `get(session_id)` - Retrieves container info for a session.
  - `stop(session_id)` - Stops and removes a container, with error handling.
  - `cleanup_expired()` - Removes containers exceeding max lifetime.
  - `shutdown_all()` - Stops all containers (for backend shutdown).

### Modified file: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/agent.py`

   from app.config import ANTHROPIC_MODEL, PROXY_BASE_URL, CONTAINER_ENABLED

2. **New function `_stream_chat_container()` (lines 106-166):** Added after `_extract_tool_result_text()` and before `stream_chat()`. This async generator function:
   - Builds the query message and system prompt
   - Creates a proxy token for the session
   - Calls `container_manager.create()` to get a sidecar container
   - Polls the container's `/health` endpoint up to 10 times (1 second apart) waiting for readiness
   - Sends the query payload to the container's `/query` endpoint via HTTP streaming
   - Yields SSE events (`data:` and `event:` lines) back to the caller

   - Always revokes the proxy token in the `finally` block

   ```python
   if CONTAINER_ENABLED:
       from app.container_manager import container_manager
       async for event in _stream_chat_container(
           message, session_id, db, conversation_history, container_manager
       ):
           yield event
       return
   ```
   The `container_manager` import is deferred so it won't fail when `CONTAINER_ENABLED` is `false` (the default), since the `container_manager` module may not exist yet (Task 9).

## Summary

**Modified file:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/main.py`

Three changes were made:

### 1. Added import (line 13)
```python
from app.config import CONTAINER_ENABLED
```

### 2. Extended `_cleanup_loop()` (lines 29-33)
Added container cleanup after the proxy cleanup section. When `CONTAINER_ENABLED` is true, the background loop (running every 60 seconds) now also calls `container_manager.cleanup_expired()` to remove containers that have exceeded their max lifetime.

```python
        if CONTAINER_ENABLED:
            from app.container_manager import container_manager
            container_removed = container_manager.cleanup_expired()
            if container_removed:
                logger.info("Background cleanup: removed %d expired containers", container_removed)
```

### 3. Extended `lifespan()` (lines 41-43)
Added container shutdown on application exit. When `CONTAINER_ENABLED` is true, `container_manager.shutdown_all()` is called to stop and remove all active containers during graceful shutdown.

```python
    if CONTAINER_ENABLED:
        from app.container_manager import container_manager
        container_manager.shutdown_all()
```

Both container integrations use deferred imports (`from app.container_manager import container_manager` inside the `if` blocks) to avoid import errors when the `docker` package is not installed. The `CONTAINER_ENABLED` guard ensures these code paths are only reached when container isolation is explicitly enabled.

Task 9 is complete. Here is a summary of what was done:

**File modified:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/container_manager.py`

**Change:** Added a module-level singleton `container_manager` instance at the bottom of the file (lines 128-149), wrapped in a `try/except` block:

```python
try:
    from app.config import (
        CONTAINER_IMAGE,
        CONTAINER_RUNTIME,
        CONTAINER_MEMORY_LIMIT,
        CONTAINER_CPU_LIMIT,
        CONTAINER_MAX_LIFETIME_SECONDS,
        CONTAINER_NETWORK,
    )

    container_manager = ContainerManager(
        ContainerConfig(
            image=CONTAINER_IMAGE,
            runtime=CONTAINER_RUNTIME,
            memory_limit=CONTAINER_MEMORY_LIMIT,
            cpu_limit=CONTAINER_CPU_LIMIT,
            REDACTED,
            network=CONTAINER_NETWORK,
        )
    )
except Exception:
    container_manager = None  # type: ignore[assignment]
```

**Rationale for try/except:** The `docker.from_env()` call in `ContainerManager.__init__` will fail if Docker is not installed. The `try/except` ensures:
- When Docker is available: the singleton is created with configuration from environment variables via `app.config`.
- When Docker is not available (e.g., test environments, non-containerized deployments): the singleton is set to `None`, which is safe because callers check `CONTAINER_ENABLED` before importing this module.
- Tests that mock Docker continue to work because they create their own `ContainerManager` instances.

**Tests:** All 38 tests pass (commit `c30e131`).

  "prompt": "You are implementing Task 10: Docker network setup script\n\n## Task Description\n\n**Files:**\n- Create: `sidecar/setup-network.sh`\n\n### Step 1: Create `sidecar/setup-network.sh`\n\n```bash\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\nset -euo pipefail\n\nNETWORK_NAME=\"${CONTAINER_NETWORK:-agent-sandbox}\"\n\n# Create the bridge network if it doesn't exist\nif ! docker network inspect \"$NETWORK_NAME\" &>/dev/null; then\n    echo \"Creating Docker network: $NETWORK_NAME\"\n    docker network create \\\n        --driver bridge \\\n        --internal=false \\\n        \"$NETWORK_NAME\"\n    echo \"Network $NETWORK_NAME created.\"\nelse\n    echo \"Network $NETWORK_NAME already exists.\"\nfi\n\necho \"\"\necho \"To block internal network access from containers, add iptables rules:\"\necho \"  iptables -I DOCKER-USER -s 172.18.0.0/16 -d 10.0.0.0/8 -j DROP\"\necho \"  iptables -I DOCKER-USER -s 172.18.0.0/16 -d 172.16.0.0/12 -j DROP\"\necho \"  iptables -I DOCKER-USER -s 172.18.0.0/16 -d 192.168.0.0/16 -j DROP\"\necho \"  iptables -I DOCKER-USER -s 172.18.0.0/16 -d 169.254.0.0/16 -j DROP\"\necho \"\"\necho \"Note: Allow the host proxy address explicitly before applying these rules.\"\n```\n\n### Step 2: Make it executable\n\nRun: `chmod +x sidecar/setup-network.sh`\n\n### Step 3: Commit\n\n```bash\ngit add sidecar/setup-network.sh\ngit commit -m \"feat(sidecar): add Docker network setup script\"\n```\n\n## Context\n\nThis script creates the Docker bridge network that sidecar containers connect to. The network allows outbound internet access but the iptables guidance helps block access to internal networks (cloud metadata, private subnets).\n\nWork from: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime`\n\n## Your Job\n\n1. **Created file:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/sidecar/setup-network.sh`

The script:
- Uses `set -euo pipefail` for strict error handling
- Reads the network name from the `CONTAINER_NETWORK` environment variable, defaulting to `agent-sandbox`
- Checks if the Docker network already exists via `docker network inspect`
- Creates a bridge network with `--internal=false` (allowing outbound internet access) if it doesn't exist
- Prints guidance on iptables rules to block access to internal/private networks (RFC 1918 ranges plus link-local)

---

## Code Review: Containerized Runtime Feature

### Scope

12 commits introducing a sidecar container architecture: a TypeScript HTTP server wrapping the Claude CLI runs inside a gVisor-sandboxed Docker container, managed by a Python `ContainerManager` in the backend, gated behind a `CONTAINER_ENABLED` feature flag.

---

### Strengths

1. **Clean feature flag isolation.** The `CONTAINER_ENABLED` flag in `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/config.py` (line 26) defaults to `false`. The dispatch in `stream_chat()` at `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/agent.py` (lines 177-183) early-returns the container path cleanly, and the existing subprocess path is completely untouched. No regressions to the existing flow.

2. **Defense-in-depth security posture.** The container runs with `read_only=True`, `cap_drop=["ALL"]`, `security_opt=["no-new-privileges"]`, and `tmpfs` limited to 50MB. The gVisor runtime adds syscall filtering. These are correct and comprehensive hardening flags in `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/container_manager.py` (lines 53-56).

3. **Credential scrubbing.** Both the container path and the existing subprocess path blank out `LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY` and `LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY` in the environment passed to the Claude CLI. The proxy token approach (short-lived UUID instead of real API key) is correctly applied in the container path at `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/agent.py` (lines 121-127).

4. **Lifecycle management.** The background cleanup loop in `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/main.py` (lines 29-33) correctly reaps expired containers every 60 seconds. The shutdown hook (lines 41-43) calls `shutdown_all()` for graceful teardown.

5. **Well-structured tests.** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/tests/test_container_manager.py` covers creation, security flags, stop, idempotent stop of nonexistent sessions, expiry, keepalive of recent containers, shutdown-all, and URL formatting. Good edge case coverage.

6. **Consistent patterns.** The implementation follows the existing codebase patterns -- dataclasses for configuration, module-level singletons, lazy imports behind feature flags, and the same SSE streaming conventions.

---

### Issues

#### Critical

**C1. Dockerfile will fail to build: `npm ci --omit=dev` excludes `typescript`, then `npx tsc` will fail.**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/sidecar/Dockerfile`, lines 17-20.

```dockerfile
RUN npm ci --omit=dev
COPY tsconfig.json ./
COPY src/ ./src/
RUN npx tsc
```

`typescript` is listed under `devDependencies` in `package.json`. With `--omit=dev`, it is not installed, so `npx tsc` will either fail or download a random version at build time (non-deterministic). This is a build blocker.

**Fix:** Use a multi-stage build. Install all dependencies in a build stage, compile TypeScript, then copy only `dist/` and production `node_modules` into the final stage. Alternatively, run `npm ci` (without `--omit=dev`) for the build step, then prune dev dependencies afterward.

---

**C2. Read-only filesystem will break Claude CLI and `mkdtempSync`.**

The container is launched with `read_only=True` and only `/tmp` mounted as tmpfs (50MB). However:

- The Claude CLI writes configuration and session state to `~/.claude/` (the home directory of `appuser` at `/home/appuser`). A read-only filesystem will cause the CLI to crash or fail to start.
- The sidecar's `mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "sidecar-"))` in `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/sidecar/src/server.ts` (line 38) writes to `/tmp`, which is writable, so that part is fine. But Claude CLI itself needs writable home directory space.

**Fix:** Add a tmpfs mount for the Claude CLI's home directory:
```python
tmpfs={"/tmp": "size=50m", "/home/appuser/.claude": "size=10m"},
```
Or alternatively mount `/home/appuser` as tmpfs.

---

**C3. MCP server URL `{PROXY_BASE_URL}/mcp` has no corresponding endpoint.**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/agent.py`, line 151:

```python
"mcp_server_url": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/mcp",
```

The sidecar passes this URL to the Claude CLI as an SSE MCP server config. But the backend has no `/mcp` route. The existing MCP server (`create_duckdb_server` in `tools.py`) is an in-process SDK server that runs as a subprocess stdio pipe -- it is not exposed as an HTTP/SSE endpoint.

In the subprocess path, the SDK manages the MCP server in-process. In the container path, the Claude CLI inside the container needs to reach the DuckDB MCP server over the network, but no such server is running.

**Fix:** This is a fundamental architecture gap. The backend needs to expose the DuckDB MCP tool as an HTTP SSE endpoint (e.g., using FastAPI + the MCP SDK's SSE transport) so the containerized Claude CLI can connect to it. Without this, the container path cannot execute SQL queries -- the core functionality is broken.

---

#### Important

**I1. `PROXY_BASE_URL` defaults to `127.0.0.1` -- unreachable from inside a container.**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/config.py`, line 14:

```python
PROXY_BASE_URL = os.getenv("PROXY_BASE_URL", "http://127.0.0.1:10000")
```

When the container runs on a Docker bridge network, `127.0.0.1` refers to the container's own loopback, not the host. The Anthropic proxy and (future) MCP endpoint would be unreachable.

**Fix:** Documentation should make it clear that `PROXY_BASE_URL` must be set to the host's Docker bridge IP (e.g., `http://172.17.0.1:10000` or `http://host.docker.internal:10000`) when `CONTAINER_ENABLED=true`. Better yet, detect this at startup and warn if `CONTAINER_ENABLED=true` and `PROXY_BASE_URL` contains `127.0.0.1` or `localhost`.

---

**I2. Sidecar server uses a global `activeProcess` singleton -- only one concurrent query per container.**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/sidecar/src/server.ts`, line 13:

```typescript
let activeProcess: ChildProcess | null = null;
```

If two `/query` requests arrive concurrently, the second one overwrites `activeProcess`, and the `/stop` endpoint can only stop the most recent one. This is acceptable if the architecture guarantees one container per session (which it does -- `ContainerManager.create` returns the existing container for the same session). However, the sidecar's `/stop` endpoint may kill the wrong process if concurrent requests arrive. This should at least be documented, or the sidecar should reject concurrent requests with a 409.

---

**I3. Container is never stopped after a query completes.**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/agent.py`, `_stream_chat_container` creates a container (line 131) but never calls `container_manager.stop()` when the query finishes. The container is only cleaned up by:
- The 60-second background cleanup loop (but only after `max_lifetime_seconds` = 600s).
- Application shutdown.

This means containers linger for up to 10 minutes after a conversation ends, consuming Docker resources. With many users, this could exhaust available containers or memory.

**Fix:** Add `container_manager.stop(container_session)` in the `finally` block of `_stream_chat_container`, or at least provide a mechanism for the frontend to signal session end. If container reuse across messages in a session is desired, then the session end signal is the right approach, but the current design has no such signal.

---

**I4. The `container_manager` singleton silently swallows initialization errors.**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/container_manager.py`, lines 128-149:

```python
try:
    from app.config import (...)
    container_manager = ContainerManager(...)
except Exception:
    container_manager = None
```

If `docker.from_env()` fails (Docker not installed, socket not accessible), `container_manager` is silently set to `None`. Then in `agent.py` line 178-179:

```python
if CONTAINER_ENABLED:
    from app.container_manager import container_manager
```

If `CONTAINER_ENABLED=true` but `container_manager is None`, calling `container_manager.create(...)` will raise `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'create'`. The error message will be unhelpful.

**Fix:** Either fail fast at startup when `CONTAINER_ENABLED=true` but Docker is unavailable, or add a guard check in `_stream_chat_container` with a clear error message.

---

**I5. No `done` event emitted in the container streaming path.**

The existing subprocess path emits `event: done\ndata: {"session_id": ...}` when the CLI finishes (line 342). The container path in `_stream_chat_container` simply forwards raw SSE lines from the sidecar but never emits its own `done` event. The sidecar server also does not emit a `done` event -- it just closes the connection after an optional `error` event.

The frontend likely depends on the `done` event to know the stream is finished and to capture the `session_id`.

**Fix:** The sidecar should emit `event: done` with the session ID from the Claude CLI's output, or the backend should synthesize one after the sidecar stream ends.

---

#### Minor

**M1. SSE event forwarding in `_stream_chat_container` may corrupt multi-line events.**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/agent.py`, lines 156-160:

```python
async for line in response.aiter_lines():
    if line.startswith("data: "):
        yield f"{line}\n\n"
    elif line.startswith("event: "):
        yield f"{line}\n"
```

SSE events are structured as `event: <name>\ndata: <payload>\n\n`. The code yields `event:` lines with a single newline and `data:` lines with double newline. But if an `event:` line precedes a `data:` line, the client will receive them as separate events. The `event:` prefix should be combined with its subsequent `data:` line before yielding. The current code works incidentally because most SSE consumers are lenient, but it does not conform to the SSE specification properly.

---

**M2. The `setup-network.sh` script creates a non-internal network.**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/sidecar/setup-network.sh`, line 12:

```bash
--internal=false \
```

With `--internal=false`, containers on this network can reach the public internet. The iptables rules to block internal network access are only printed as suggestions, not applied. For a security-focused feature, the network should default to `--internal=true` and explicitly allow only the host proxy IP. The current approach relies on manual operator action.

---

**M3. `test_agent_container.py` is trivially weak.**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/tests/test_agent_container.py`:

```python
def test_container_enabled_reads_from_config():
    assert CONTAINER_ENABLED is False or CONTAINER_ENABLED is True
```

This test passes for any boolean value and does not actually verify the feature flag behavior (e.g., that it defaults to `False`, or that setting the env var to `"true"` makes it `True`). It adds no meaningful coverage.

---

**M4. Sidecar process environment leaks all host env vars into `claude` subprocess.**

File: `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/sidecar/src/server.ts`, line 73:

```typescript
env: process.env as Record<string, string>,
```

The sidecar passes its entire `process.env` to the Claude subprocess. While the container's environment is controlled by `ContainerManager.create`, if any unexpected env vars leak into the container (e.g., from the Docker image build), they would propagate to the CLI. A more defensive approach would be to pass only the explicitly needed variables (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`).

---

**M5. No input validation or size limit on the sidecar `/query` endpoint beyond Express's 10MB JSON limit.**

The `system_prompt` and `message` fields are passed directly to the Claude CLI as command-line arguments. Extremely long values could hit OS argument length limits (typically 128KB-2MB). The 10MB Express body limit is far above this threshold.

---

### Overall Assessment

The feature is **architecturally sound** in its design -- the sidecar pattern, the feature flag, the security hardening, and the lifecycle management are all well-conceived and follow the existing codebase conventions cleanly. The non-container path is completely unaffected.

However, there are **three critical issues that would prevent this from working in practice**:

1. The Dockerfile will not build (C1 -- `npm ci --omit=dev` then `npx tsc`).
2. The read-only filesystem will break the Claude CLI at runtime (C2 -- no writable home directory).
3. The MCP server is not exposed as an HTTP endpoint (C3 -- the containerized CLI has no way to execute SQL queries).

Additionally, the `PROXY_BASE_URL` default of `127.0.0.1` is unreachable from containers (I1), containers are never stopped after queries (I3), the `done` event is missing (I5), and the `container_manager` singleton can be `None` at runtime without a clear error (I4).

**Recommendation:** The critical issues (C1, C2, C3) and important issues (I1, I3, I4, I5) should be resolved before merging. The feature flag ensures zero risk to the existing path, but the containerized path would not be functional as implemented.

---

## Summary of Changes

### C1: Dockerfile multi-stage build (`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/sidecar/Dockerfile`)

The original Dockerfile ran `npm ci --omit=dev` (which excludes TypeScript) then tried `npx tsc`, which would fail. Replaced with a two-stage build:
- **Build stage**: installs all dependencies (including devDependencies), runs `npx tsc` to compile TypeScript to `dist/`.
- **Production stage**: installs only production `node_modules` via `npm ci --omit=dev`, copies the compiled `dist/` from the build stage using `COPY --from=build /app/dist ./dist`.

### C2: tmpfs mount for Claude CLI home directory (`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/container_manager.py`)

The container runs with `read_only=True`, but the Claude CLI needs to write to `~/.claude/`. Added a tmpfs mount for `/home/appuser/.claude` (20MB) alongside the existing `/tmp` mount.

### C3: MCP server TODO (`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/agent.py`)

Removed the `mcp_server_url` field from the sidecar query payload (the endpoint does not exist yet) and added a TODO comment explaining the architecture gap: the MCP server needs to be exposed as an HTTP SSE endpoint for the containerized CLI to reach DuckDB.

### I1: PROXY_BASE_URL localhost warning (`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/agent.py`)

Added a `logger.warning()` call before container creation that fires when `PROXY_BASE_URL` contains `127.0.0.1` or `localhost`, advising the user to set it to a Docker-accessible address like `http://host.docker.internal:10000`.

### I3: Container lifecycle documentation (`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/agent.py`)

Added a comment in the `finally` block of `_stream_chat_container()` explaining that containers are intentionally kept alive for session resume via the `--resume` flag, and are cleaned up by the background cleanup loop or on application shutdown.

### I4: Graceful fallback when Docker unavailable (`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/agent.py`)

Added a `None` check for `container_manager` in `stream_chat()`. When `CONTAINER_ENABLED=true` but Docker is not available (so `container_manager is None`), it logs an error and falls through to the subprocess mode instead of crashing with an `AttributeError`.

### I5: Done event in container streaming path (`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/agent.py`)

Added a `yield` of the `event: done` SSE event with the `session_id` after the sidecar streaming loop completes successfully, matching the behavior of the subprocess streaming path.
