## Report: MCP (Model Context Protocol) SSE Transport Architecture

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here's a complete analysis of how MCP SSE transport works and how it's currently used in this project.

---

## 1. CURRENT MCP USAGE (In-Process Subprocess Model)

### Backend Implementation
**File:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/tools.py`

The DuckDB tool is currently defined as an **in-process MCP server** using the Claude SDK:

```python
from claude_agent_sdk import tool, create_sdk_mcp_server

def create_duckdb_server(db: Database):
    @tool(
        "execute_sql",
        "Execute a SQL query against the DuckDB database...",
        {"sql": str},
    )
    async def execute_sql(args: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
        sql = args["sql"]
        result = db.execute_query(sql)
        return {"content": [{"type": "text", "text": json.dumps(result_json)}]}

    return create_sdk_mcp_server(
        name="duckdb",
        version="1.0.0",
        tools=[execute_sql],
    )
```

### Integration in Agent
**File:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/backend/app/agent.py:210-243`

The subprocess path passes the SDK MCP server directly to the Claude CLI:

```python
duckdb_server = create_duckdb_server(db)

options = ClaudeAgentOptions(
    model=ANTHROPIC_MODEL,
    system_prompt=build_system_prompt(db),
    mcp_servers={"duckdb": duckdb_server},      # In-process server
    allowed_tools=["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"],
    permission_mode="bypassPermissions",
    max_turns=20,
    include_partial_messages=True,
    env={...}
)

client = ClaudeSDKClient(options=options)
```

The SDK automatically handles the subprocess communication internally.

---

## 2. WHAT THE SIDECAR EXPECTS FOR MCP

**File:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/sidecar/src/server.ts`

The sidecar is **designed to receive an SSE URL** for the MCP server via the `/query` payload:

```typescript
export interface QueryRequest {
  message: string;
  session_id?: string;
  system_prompt: string;
  model?: string;
  mcp_server_url?: string;  // Optional SSE URL
}

app.post("/query", (req: Request, res: Response) => {
  const body = req.body as QueryRequest;

  // Write MCP config to temp file if mcp_server_url provided
  let mcpConfigPath: string | null = null;
  if (body.mcp_server_url) {
    const tmpDir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "sidecar-"));
    mcpConfigPath = join(tmpDir, "mcp.json");
    writeFileSync(
      mcpConfigPath,
      JSON.stringify({
        mcpServers: {
          duckdb: {
            type: "sse",
            url: body.mcp_server_url,
          },
        },
      })
    );
  }

  const args = [
    "--mcp-config", mcpConfigPath,  // Pass config file to Claude CLI
    ...
  ];

  const proc = spawn("claude", args, {...});
});
```

### Current Issue
**Design doc annotation in agent.py (line 159-162):**
```python
# TODO: MCP server needs to be exposed as HTTP SSE endpoint
# for containerized CLI to reach DuckDB. Currently the tool
# is defined in-process via the SDK. This requires implementing
# an MCP SSE transport on the backend. See design doc for details.
```

---

## 3. MCP SDK/LIBRARY OPTIONS FOR PYTHON

### Available Transports
The project has access to the **mcp package** (v0.1.38+) which provides multiple transport options:

#### A. **In-Process SDK** (Current)
- `claude_agent_sdk.create_sdk_mcp_server()` - SDK-native server
- Tools run in Python directly
- Returns `McpSdkServerConfig(type="sdk", instance=server)`
- **Used for:** Local subprocess path

#### B. **SSE Transport** (For HTTP)
- **Server:** `mcp.server.sse.SseServerTransport`
- **Client:** `mcp.client.sse.sse_client` (async context manager)
- Returns `McpSSEServerConfig(type="sse", url="http://...")`
- Requires Starlette + Uvicorn or similar ASGI framework
- **Location:** `backend/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/mcp/server/sse.py`

#### C. **Streamable HTTP Transport**
- **Client:** `mcp.client.streamable_http.StreamableHTTPTransport`
- POST-based HTTP with optional SSE streaming
- Returns `McpHttpServerConfig(type="http", url="http://...")`
- **Location:** `backend/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/mcp/client/streamable_http.py`

#### D. **Stdio Transport**
- `McpStdioServerConfig(type="stdio", command="...", args=[...])`
- Spawns process and communicates via stdin/stdout
- Not needed for this architecture

### Recommended Option: **SSE Transport**
The `SseServerTransport` is ideal because:
1. Matches the Claude CLI's `--mcp-config` format expectations
2. Two-endpoint architecture fits cleanly with HTTP server
3. Proven to work with external MCP clients
4. Already in dependencies via `mcp` package

---

## 4. HOW CLAUDE CLI CONFIGURES MCP SERVERS (--mcp-config Format)

### MCP Config File Format
The Claude CLI accepts `--mcp-config <path>` pointing to a JSON file:

```json
{
  "mcpServers": {
    "duckdb": {
      "type": "sse",
      "url": "http://host.docker.internal:10000/mcp/sse"
    }
  }
}
```

### Supported Types
From `claude_agent_sdk/types.py`:

```python
# Stdio process
McpStdioServerConfig:
  type: "stdio"
  command: str          # e.g., "python -m mcp.server.stdio"
  args: list[str]       # optional
  env: dict[str, str]   # optional environment variables

# Server-Sent Events
McpSSEServerConfig:
  type: "sse"
  url: str              # e.g., "http://localhost:3000/sse"
  headers: dict         # optional, for auth headers

# HTTP POST-based
McpHttpServerConfig:
  type: "http"
  url: str              # e.g., "http://localhost:3000"
  headers: dict         # optional

# SDK In-Process (Python only)
McpSdkServerConfig:
  type: "sdk"
  name: str
  instance: McpServer   # Actual Python object
```

### Sidecar Implementation
The sidecar writes this config and spawns Claude CLI with:
```bash
claude --mcp-config /tmp/mcp.json --output-format stream-json ...
```

---

## 5. SSE TRANSPORT TECHNICAL DETAILS

### Server-Side (Producer)
**File:** `backend/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/mcp/server/sse.py`

```python
class SseServerTransport:
    """Provides two ASGI endpoints:
    1. GET /sse endpoint - bidirectional SSE connection
    2. POST /messages endpoint - client messages via JSON POST
    """
    
    def __init__(self, endpoint: str, security_settings=None):
        # endpoint: relative path where client POSTs messages
        self._endpoint = "/messages/"  # e.g., "/api/mcp/messages"
```

**Protocol Flow:**
1. Client opens SSE connection to `GET /sse`
2. Server streams `{"event": "endpoint", "data": "/messages?session_id=..."}` 
3. Client knows where to POST messages
4. Server streams `{"event": "message", "data": "{...json-rpc...}"}` for responses
5. Client POSTs client messages to the endpoint URL

### Client-Side (Consumer)
**File:** `backend/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/mcp/client/sse.py`

```python
@asynccontextmanager
async def sse_client(
    url: str,
    headers: dict | None = None,
    timeout: float = 5,
    sse_read_timeout: float = 60 * 5,
    auth: httpx.Auth | None = None,
    on_session_created: Callable | None = None,
):
    """
    Expects:
    - GET connection to url (SSE endpoint)
    - Receives "endpoint" event with POST URL
    - Receives "message" events with JSONRPC responses
    - POSTs client messages to the endpoint URL
    
    Yields: (read_stream, write_stream) for communication
    """
```

### Security & Session Management
- Each SSE connection gets a unique `session_id` (UUID)
- POST requests must include `?session_id=<uuid>` to link to correct stream
- DNS rebinding protection built-in via `TransportSecurityMiddleware`
- Headers validated for origin matching

---

## 6. WHAT CHANGES ARE NEEDED TO EXPOSE DUCKDB OVER HTTP

### Architecture Overview
```
┌──────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Backend FastAPI Application          │
│                                      │
│  ┌────────────────────────────────┐  │
│  │ New MCP SSE Endpoint Handler   │  │
│  │                                │  │
│  │  GET /mcp/sse                  │  │
│  │  POST /mcp/messages            │  │
│  └────────────────────────────────┘  │
│           │                           │
│           ├─ Creates SseServerTransport
│           ├─ Wraps DuckDB MCP server
│           └─ Streams to sidecar      │
│                                      │
│  DuckDB Instance (existing)          │
└──────────────────────────────────────┘
         ↑
         │ (sidecar sends HTTP requests)
         │
┌──────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Docker Container (Sidecar)           │
│                                      │
│  Claude CLI                          │
│  (configured with --mcp-config)      │
└──────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### Implementation Steps

#### Step 1: Create MCP SSE Endpoint in Backend
Add to `backend/app/main.py` (or new module `backend/app/mcp_server.py`):

```python
from mcp.server.sse import SseServerTransport
from starlette.responses import Response
from fastapi import Request

class DuckDBMCPManager:
    def __init__(self, db: Database):
        self.db = db
        self.sse_transport = SseServerTransport(endpoint="/mcp/messages/")
        # Would wrap existing DuckDB tools here
    
    async def sse_handler(self, scope, receive, send):
        """GET /mcp/sse - SSE connection endpoint"""
        # run MCP server logic here
        async with self.sse_transport.connect_sse(scope, receive, send):
            streams = ...  # Run DuckDB MCP server
            await app.run(streams[0], streams[1], ...)
        return Response()
    
    async def messages_handler(self, request: Request):
        """POST /mcp/messages - Handle client messages"""
        return await self.sse_transport.handle_post_message(
            request.scope, request.receive, request._send
        )
```

#### Step 2: Wire Routes
```python
@app.get("/mcp/sse")
async def mcp_sse(request: Request):
    return await duckdb_mcp_manager.sse_handler(
        request.scope, request.receive, request._send
    )

@app.post("/mcp/messages")
async def mcp_messages(request: Request):
    return await duckdb_mcp_manager.messages_handler(request)
```

#### Step 3: Update Sidecar to Use SSE URL
Modify `backend/app/agent.py` (_stream_chat_container):

```python
async def _stream_chat_container(...):
    payload = {
        "message": query_message,
        "session_id": session_id,
        "system_prompt": system_prompt,
        "model": ANTHROPIC_MODEL,
        "mcp_server_url": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/mcp/sse",  # Pass URL
    }
    
    async with httpx.AsyncClient() as client:
        async with client.stream("POST", f"{info.url}/query", json=payload):
            ...
```

#### Step 4: Ensure Network Access
- Sidecar container must reach `host.docker.internal:{backend_port}`
- Already configured in design doc (line 142):
  ```
  ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL: http://host.docker.internal:10000/anthropic
  ```
- MCP endpoint would be on same host: `http://host.docker.internal:10000/mcp/sse`

---

- DNS rebinding protection

## 8. SIDECAR DEPENDENCIES

**File:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/sidecar/package.json`

```json
{
  "dependencies": {
    "express": "^4.21.0"
  },
  "devDependencies": {
    "@types/express": "^5.0.0",
    "@types/node": "^22.0.0",
    "tsx": "^4.0.0",
    "typescript": "^5.7.0"
  }
}
```

**Note:** The sidecar currently doesn't need to understand MCP. It:
1. Receives `mcp_server_url` in the query payload
2. Writes it to a config file as `--mcp-config`
3. Spawns `claude` CLI which handles SSE client connection
4. The CLI internally uses the `mcp` package to connect via SSE

---

## 9. DESIGN DOC REFERENCE

**File:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/containerized-runtime/docs/plans/2026-02-22-containerized-runtime-design.md`

Key architecture points (lines 72-82):

```
6. Claude CLI's MCP tool calls go to host DuckDB via host network
7. Sidecar streams SSE events back to backend
8. Backend forwards SSE events to frontend (unchanged format)
9. On session end, container is stopped and removed; UUID token is revoked
```

Line 143 documents expected environment variable:
```
MCP_SERVER_URL — host MCP endpoint URL for DuckDB tool
```

But this is **not yet implemented** - currently the comment at line 159-162 of `agent.py` marks it as a TODO.

---

## SUMMARY TABLE

| Aspect | Current (Subprocess) | Future (Containerized) |
|--------|----------------------|------------------------|
| **MCP Server Type** | `McpSdkServerConfig` (in-process) | `McpSSEServerConfig` (HTTP) |
| **Location** | Backend Python process | Backend HTTP endpoint |
| **Transport** | In-memory (SDK internal) | SSE over HTTP |
| **Claude CLI Config** | Passed via `mcp_servers={}` dict | Written to JSON file + `--mcp-config` |
| **Endpoint** | N/A (in-process) | `GET /mcp/sse`, `POST /mcp/messages` |
| **Sidecar Knowledge** | None (automatic) | Receives `mcp_server_url` in payload |
| **Security** | Process isolation only | Process + container + network isolation |
| **Dependencies** | `mcp`, `claude-agent-sdk` | Same (already present) |

| Path | Purpose |

| `docs/plans/2026-02-22-containerized-runtime-design.md` | Architecture design document |

This architecture provides **multi-tenant isolation** while maintaining **identical external API** - both subprocess and container paths produce the same SSE stream for the frontend.

     1→"""
     2→SSE Server Transport Module
     3→
     4→This module implements a Server-Sent Events (SSE) transport layer for MCP servers.
     5→
     6→Example usage:
     7→```
     8→    # Create an SSE transport at an endpoint
     9→    sse = SseServerTransport("/messages/")
    10→
    11→    # Create Starlette routes for SSE and message handling
    12→    routes = [
    13→        Route("/sse", endpoint=handle_sse, methods=["GET"]),
    14→        Mount("/messages/", app=sse.handle_post_message),
    15→    ]
    16→
    17→    # Define handler functions
    18→    async def handle_sse(request):
    19→        async with sse.connect_sse(
    20→            request.scope, request.receive, request._send
    21→        ) as streams:
    22→            await app.run(
    23→                streams[0], streams[1], app.create_initialization_options()
    24→            )
    25→        # Return empty response to avoid NoneType error
    26→        return Response()
    27→
    28→    # Create and run Starlette app
    29→    starlette_app = Starlette(routes=routes)
    30→    uvicorn.run(starlette_app, host="127.0.0.1", port=port)
    31→```
    32→
    33→Note: The handle_sse function must return a Response to avoid a "TypeError: 'NoneType'
    34→object is not callable" error when client disconnects. The example above returns
    35→an empty Response() after the SSE connection ends to fix this.
    36→
    37→See SseServerTransport class documentation for more details.
    38→"""
    39→
    40→import logging
    41→from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
    42→from typing import Any
    43→from urllib.parse import quote
    44→from uuid import UUID, uuid4
    45→
    46→import anyio
    47→from anyio.streams.memory import MemoryObjectReceiveStream, MemoryObjectSendStream
    48→from pydantic import ValidationError
    49→from sse_starlette import EventSourceResponse
    50→from starlette.requests import Request
    51→from starlette.responses import Response
    52→from starlette.types import Receive, Scope, Send
    53→
    54→import mcp.types as types
    55→from mcp.server.transport_security import (
    56→    TransportSecurityMiddleware,
    57→    TransportSecuritySettings,
    58→)
    59→from mcp.shared.message import ServerMessageMetadata, SessionMessage
    60→
    61→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    62→
    63→
    64→class SseServerTransport:
    65→    """
    66→    SSE server transport for MCP. This class provides _two_ ASGI applications,
    67→    suitable to be used with a framework like Starlette and a server like Hypercorn:
    68→
    69→        1. connect_sse() is an ASGI application which receives incoming GET requests,
    70→           and sets up a new SSE stream to send server messages to the client.
    71→        2. handle_post_message() is an ASGI application which receives incoming POST
    72→           requests, which should contain client messages that link to a
    73→           previously-established SSE session.
    74→    """
    75→
    76→    _endpoint: str
    77→    _read_stream_writers: dict[UUID, MemoryObjectSendStream[SessionMessage | Exception]]
    78→    _security: TransportSecurityMiddleware
    79→
    80→    def __init__(self, endpoint: str, security_settings: TransportSecuritySettings | None = None) -> None:
    81→        """
    82→        Creates a new SSE server transport, which will direct the client to POST
    83→        messages to the relative path given.
    84→
    85→        Args:
    86→            endpoint: A relative path where messages should be posted
    87→                    (e.g., "/messages/").
    88→            security_settings: Optional security settings for DNS rebinding protection.
    89→
    90→        Note:
    91→            We use relative paths instead of full URLs for several reasons:
    92→            1. Security: Prevents cross-origin requests by ensuring clients only connect
    93→               to the same origin they established the SSE connection with
    94→            2. Flexibility: The server can be mounted at any path without needing to
    95→               know its full URL
    96→            3. Portability: The same endpoint configuration works across different
    97→               environments (development, staging, production)
    98→
    99→        Raises:
   100→            ValueError: If the endpoint is a full URL instead of a relative path
   101→        """
   102→
   103→        super().__init__()
   104→
   105→        # Validate that endpoint is a relative path and not a full URL
   106→        if "://" in endpoint or endpoint.startswith("//") or "?" in endpoint or "#" in endpoint:
   107→            raise ValueError(
   108→                f"Given endpoint: {endpoint} is not a relative path (e.g., '/messages/'), "
   109→                "expecting a relative path (e.g., '/messages/')."
   110→            )
   111→
   112→        # Ensure endpoint starts with a forward slash
   113→        if not endpoint.startswith("/"):
   114→            endpoint = "/" + endpoint
   115→
   116→        self._endpoint = endpoint
   117→        self._read_stream_writers = {}
   118→        self._security = TransportSecurityMiddleware(security_settings)
   119→        logger.debug(f"SseServerTransport initialized with endpoint: {endpoint}")
   120→
   121→    @asynccontextmanager
   122→    async def connect_sse(self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send):  # pragma: no cover
   123→        if scope["type"] != "http":
   124→            logger.error("connect_sse received non-HTTP request")
   125→            raise ValueError("connect_sse can only handle HTTP requests")
   126→
   127→        # Validate request headers for DNS rebinding protection
   128→        request = Request(scope, receive)
   129→        error_response = await self._security.validate_request(request, is_post=False)
   130→        if error_response:
   131→            await error_response(scope, receive, send)
   132→            raise ValueError("Request validation failed")
   133→
   134→        logger.debug("Setting up SSE connection")
   135→        read_stream: MemoryObjectReceiveStream[SessionMessage | Exception]
   136→        read_stream_writer: MemoryObjectSendStream[SessionMessage | Exception]
   137→
   138→        write_stream: MemoryObjectSendStream[SessionMessage]
   139→        write_stream_reader: MemoryObjectReceiveStream[SessionMessage]
   140→
   141→        read_stream_writer, read_stream = anyio.create_memory_object_stream(0)
   142→        write_stream, write_stream_reader = anyio.create_memory_object_stream(0)
   143→
   144→        session_id = uuid4()
   145→        self._read_stream_writers[session_id] = read_stream_writer
   146→        logger.debug(f"Created new session with ID: {session_id}")
   147→
   148→        # Determine the full path for the message endpoint to be sent to the client.
   149→        # scope['root_path'] is the prefix where the current Starlette app
   150→        # instance is mounted.
   151→        # e.g., "" if top-level, or "/api_prefix" if mounted under "/api_prefix".
   152→        root_path = scope.get("root_path", "")
   153→
   154→        # self._endpoint is the path *within* this app, e.g., "/messages".
   155→        # Concatenating them gives the full absolute path from the server root.
   156→        # e.g., "" + "/messages" -> "/messages"
   157→        # e.g., "/api_prefix" + "/messages" -> "/api_prefix/messages"
   158→        full_message_path_for_client = root_path.rstrip("/") + self._endpoint
   159→
   160→        # This is the URI (path + query) the client will use to POST messages.
   161→        client_post_uri_data = f"{quote(full_message_path_for_client)}?session_id={session_id.hex}"
   162→
   163→        sse_stream_writer, sse_stream_reader = anyio.create_memory_object_stream[dict[str, Any]](0)
   164→
   165→        async def sse_writer():
   166→            logger.debug("Starting SSE writer")
   167→            async with sse_stream_writer, write_stream_reader:
   168→                await sse_stream_writer.send({"event": "endpoint", "data": client_post_uri_data})
   169→                logger.debug(f"Sent endpoint event: {client_post_uri_data}")
   170→
   171→                async for session_message in write_stream_reader:
   172→                    logger.debug(f"Sending message via SSE: {session_message}")
   173→                    await sse_stream_writer.send(
   174→                        {
   175→                            "event": "message",
   176→                            "data": session_message.message.model_dump_json(by_alias=True, exclude_none=True),
   177→                        }
   178→                    )
   179→
   180→        async with anyio.create_task_group() as tg:
   181→
   182→            async def response_wrapper(scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send):
   183→                """
   184→                The EventSourceResponse returning signals a client close / disconnect.
   185→                In this case we close our side of the streams to signal the client that
   186→                the connection has been closed.
   187→                """
   188→                await EventSourceResponse(content=sse_stream_reader, data_sender_callable=sse_writer)(
   189→                    scope, receive, send
   190→                )
   191→                await read_stream_writer.aclose()
   192→                await write_stream_reader.aclose()
   193→                logging.debug(f"Client session disconnected {session_id}")
   194→
   195→            logger.debug("Starting SSE response task")
   196→            tg.start_soon(response_wrapper, scope, receive, send)
   197→
   198→            logger.debug("Yielding read and write streams")
   199→            yield (read_stream, write_stream)
   200→
   201→    async def handle_post_message(self, scope: Scope, receive: Receive, send: Send) -> None:  # pragma: no cover
   202→        logger.debug("Handling POST message")
   203→        request = Request(scope, receive)
   204→
   205→        # Validate request headers for DNS rebinding protection
   206→        error_response = await self._security.validate_request(request, is_post=True)
   207→        if error_response:
   208→            return await error_response(scope, receive, send)
   209→
   210→        session_id_param = request.query_params.get("session_id")
   211→        if session_id_param is None:
   212→            logger.warning("Received request without session_id")
   213→            response = Response("session_id is required", status_code=400)
   214→            return await response(scope, receive, send)
   215→
   216→        try:
   217→            session_id = UUID(hex=session_id_param)
   218→            logger.debug(f"Parsed session ID: {session_id}")
   219→        except ValueError:
   220→            logger.warning(f"Received invalid session ID: {session_id_param}")
   221→            response = Response("Invalid session ID", status_code=400)
   222→            return await response(scope, receive, send)
   223→
   224→        writer = self._read_stream_writers.get(session_id)
   225→        if not writer:
   226→            logger.warning(f"Could not find session for ID: {session_id}")
   227→            response = Response("Could not find session", status_code=404)
   228→            return await response(scope, receive, send)
   229→
   230→        body = await request.body()
   231→        logger.debug(f"Received JSON: {body}")
   232→
   233→        try:
   234→            message = types.JSONRPCMessage.model_validate_json(body)
   235→            logger.debug(f"Validated client message: {message}")
   236→        except ValidationError as err:
   237→            logger.exception("Failed to parse message")
   238→            response = Response("Could not parse message", status_code=400)
   239→            await response(scope, receive, send)
   240→            await writer.send(err)
   241→            return
   242→
   243→        # Pass the ASGI scope for framework-agnostic access to request data
   244→        metadata = ServerMessageMetadata(request_context=request)
   245→        session_message = SessionMessage(message, metadata=metadata)
   246→        logger.debug(f"Sending session message to writer: {session_message}")
   247→        response = Response("Accepted", status_code=202)
   248→        await response(scope, receive, send)
   249→        await writer.send(session_message)
   250→

     1→"""
     2→MCP Server Module
     3→
     4→This module provides a framework for creating an MCP (Model Context Protocol) server.
     5→It allows you to easily define and handle various types of requests and notifications
     6→in an asynchronous manner.
     7→
     8→Usage:
     9→1. Create a Server instance:
    10→   server = Server("your_server_name")
    11→
    12→2. Define request handlers using decorators:
    13→   @server.list_prompts()
    14→   async def handle_list_prompts(request: types.ListPromptsRequest) -> types.ListPromptsResult:
    15→       # Implementation
    16→
    17→   @server.get_prompt()
    18→   async def handle_get_prompt(
    19→       name: str, arguments: dict[str, str] | None
    20→   ) -> types.GetPromptResult:
    21→       # Implementation
    22→
    23→   @server.list_tools()
    24→   async def handle_list_tools(request: types.ListToolsRequest) -> types.ListToolsResult:
    25→       # Implementation
    26→
    27→   @server.call_tool()
    28→   async def handle_call_tool(
    29→       name: str, arguments: dict | None
    30→   ) -> list[types.TextContent | types.ImageContent | types.EmbeddedResource]:
    31→       # Implementation
    32→
    33→   @server.list_resource_templates()
    34→   async def handle_list_resource_templates() -> list[types.ResourceTemplate]:
    35→       # Implementation
    36→
    37→3. Define notification handlers if needed:
    38→   @server.progress_notification()
    39→   async def handle_progress(
    40→       progress_token: str | int, progress: float, total: float | None,
    41→       message: str | None
    42→   ) -> None:
    43→       # Implementation
    44→
    45→4. Run the server:
    46→   async def main():
    47→       async with mcp.server.stdio.stdio_server() as (read_stream, write_stream):
    48→           await server.run(
    49→               read_stream,
    50→               write_stream,
    51→               InitializationOptions(
    52→                   server_name="your_server_name",
    53→                   server_version="your_version",
    54→                   capabilities=server.get_capabilities(
    55→                       notification_options=NotificationOptions(),
    56→                       experimental_capabilities={},
    57→                   ),
    58→               ),
    59→           )
    60→
    61→   asyncio.run(main())
    62→
    63→The Server class provides methods to register handlers for various MCP requests and
    64→notifications. It automatically manages the request context and handles incoming
    65→messages from the client.
    66→"""

{
  "file_path": "/Users/cyyeh/.claude/plans/wobbly-cooking-breeze.md",
  "content": "# MCP SSE Endpoint for Containerized Runtime\n\n## Context\n\nThe containerized Claude Code runtime (feature branch `feature/containerized-runtime`) has a known gap: the sidecar container's Claude CLI cannot execute SQL queries because the DuckDB MCP server only runs in-process via the Claude Agent SDK. The containerized CLI needs an HTTP-accessible MCP endpoint to reach DuckDB on the host.\n\nCurrently, `tools.py` creates an in-process MCP server via `create_sdk_mcp_server()`, which only works when the Claude subprocess runs on the same host. The sidecar container spawns `claude` CLI with `--mcp-config` pointing to an SSE URL — but no such endpoint exists.\n\n## Approach\n\nUse the `mcp` package's `SseServerTransport` (already available as a transitive dependency) to expose the DuckDB `execute_sql` tool as an SSE endpoint at `/mcp/sse` + `/mcp/messages/`. The Claude CLI inside the sidecar container connects to this endpoint via `--mcp-config`.\n\n**Key design decision:** Each SSE connection receives a `session_id` query parameter to route tool calls to the correct per-user DuckDB instance via the existing `SessionManager`.\n\n## Files to change\n\n| File | Action | Purpose |\n|------|--------|---------|\n| `backend/app/mcp_sse.py` | Create | MCP SSE endpoint: Starlette app with SSE transport |\n| `backend/app/main.py` | Modify | Mount the MCP SSE app at `/mcp` |\n| `backend/app/agent.py` | Modify | Pass `mcp_server_url` to sidecar with session_id |\n| `backend/tests/test_mcp_sse.py` | Create | Unit tests for MCP SSE endpoint |\n\n## Implementation\n\n### Task 1: Create `backend/app/mcp_sse.py`\n\nNew module implementing the MCP SSE endpoint using `mcp.server.lowlevel.server.Server` + `SseServerTransport`.\n\n```python\nimport json\nimport logging\nfrom starlette.applications import Starlette\nfrom starlette.requests import Request\nfrom starlette.responses import Response\nfrom starlette.routing import Route, Mount\nfrom mcp.server.sse import SseServerTransport\nfrom mcp.server.lowlevel.server import Server as MCPServer\nimport mcp.types as types\nfrom app.session_manager import session_manager\nfrom app.database import Database\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\nMAX_RESULT_ROWS = 100\n\nsse_transport = SseServerTransport(\"/mcp/messages/\")\n\n\ndef _create_mcp_server(db: Database) -> MCPServer:\n    \"\"\"Create an MCP server with execute_sql tool bound to a DuckDB instance.\"\"\"\n    server = MCPServer(\"duckdb\")\n\n    @server.list_tools()\n    async def list_tools() -> list[types.Tool]:\n        return [\n            types.Tool(\n                name=\"execute_sql\",\n                description=(\n                    \"Execute a SQL query against the DuckDB database. \"\n                    \"Results are returned as JSON with columns, rows, and rowCount.\"\n                ),\n                inputSchema={\n                    \"type\": \"object\",\n                    \"properties\": {\"sql\": {\"type\": \"string\"}},\n                    \"required\": [\"sql\"],\n                },\n            )\n        ]\n\n    @server.call_tool()\n    async def call_tool(\n        name: str, arguments: dict\n    ) -> list[types.TextContent]:\n        if name != \"execute_sql\":\n            raise ValueError(f\"Unknown tool: {name}\")\n        sql = arguments.get(\"sql\", \"\")\n        try:\n            result = db.execute_query(sql)\n            truncated_rows = result[\"rows\"][:MAX_RESULT_ROWS]\n            result_json = {\n                \"status\": \"success\",\n                \"columns\": result[\"columns\"],\n                \"rows\": truncated_rows,\n                \"rowCount\": result[\"rowCount\"],\n            }\n            return [types.TextContent(type=\"text\", text=json.dumps(result_json, default=str))]\n        except Exception as e:\n            error_json = {\"status\": \"error\", \"error\": str(e)}\n            return [types.TextContent(type=\"text\", text=json.dumps(error_json))]\n\n    return server\n\n\nasync def handle_sse(request: Request) -> Response:\n    \"\"\"Handle SSE connection.      1→# MCP SSE Endpoint for Containerized Runtime
     2→
     3→## Context
     4→
     5→The containerized Claude Code runtime (feature branch `feature/containerized-runtime`) has a known gap: the sidecar container's Claude CLI cannot execute SQL queries because the DuckDB MCP server only runs in-process via the Claude Agent SDK. The containerized CLI needs an HTTP-accessible MCP endpoint to reach DuckDB on the host.
     6→
     7→Currently, `tools.py` creates an in-process MCP server via `create_sdk_mcp_server()`, which only works when the Claude subprocess runs on the same host. The sidecar container spawns `claude` CLI with `--mcp-config` pointing to an SSE URL — but no such endpoint exists.
     8→
     9→## Approach
    10→
    11→Use the `mcp` package's `SseServerTransport` (already available as a transitive dependency) to expose the DuckDB `execute_sql` tool as an SSE endpoint at `/mcp/sse` + `/mcp/messages/`. The Claude CLI inside the sidecar container connects to this endpoint via `--mcp-config`.
    12→
    13→**Key design decision:** Each SSE connection receives a `session_id` query parameter to route tool calls to the correct per-user DuckDB instance via the existing `SessionManager`.
    14→
    15→## Files to change
    16→
    17→| File | Action | Purpose |
    18→|------|--------|---------|
    19→| `backend/app/mcp_sse.py` | Create | MCP SSE endpoint: Starlette app with SSE transport |
    20→| `backend/app/main.py` | Modify | Mount the MCP SSE app at `/mcp` |
    21→| `backend/app/agent.py` | Modify | Pass `mcp_server_url` to sidecar with session_id |
    22→| `backend/tests/test_mcp_sse.py` | Create | Unit tests for MCP SSE endpoint |
    23→
    24→## Implementation
    25→
    26→### Task 1: Create `backend/app/mcp_sse.py`
    27→
    28→New module implementing the MCP SSE endpoint using `mcp.server.lowlevel.server.Server` + `SseServerTransport`.
    29→
    30→```python
    31→import json
    32→import logging
    33→from starlette.applications import Starlette
    34→from starlette.requests import Request
    35→from starlette.responses import Response
    36→from starlette.routing import Route, Mount
    37→from mcp.server.sse import SseServerTransport
    38→from mcp.server.lowlevel.server import Server as MCPServer
    39→import mcp.types as types
    40→from app.session_manager import session_manager
    41→from app.database import Database
    42→
    43→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    44→
    45→MAX_RESULT_ROWS = 100
    46→
    47→sse_transport = SseServerTransport("/mcp/messages/")
    48→
    49→
    50→def _create_mcp_server(db: Database) -> MCPServer:
    51→    """Create an MCP server with execute_sql tool bound to a DuckDB instance."""
    52→    server = MCPServer("duckdb")
    53→
    54→    @server.list_tools()
    55→    async def list_tools() -> list[types.Tool]:
    56→        return [
    57→            types.Tool(
    58→                name="execute_sql",
    59→                description=(
    60→                    "Execute a SQL query against the DuckDB database. "
    61→                    "Results are returned as JSON with columns, rows, and rowCount."
    62→                ),
    63→                inputSchema={
    64→                    "type": "object",
    65→                    "properties": {"sql": {"type": "string"}},
    66→                    "required": ["sql"],
    67→                },
    68→            )
    69→        ]
    70→
    71→    @server.call_tool()
    72→    async def call_tool(
    73→        name: str, arguments: dict
    74→    ) -> list[types.TextContent]:
    75→        if name != "execute_sql":
    76→            raise ValueError(f"Unknown tool: {name}")
    77→        sql = arguments.get("sql", "")
    78→        try:
    79→            result = db.execute_query(sql)
    80→            truncated_rows = result["rows"][:MAX_RESULT_ROWS]
    81→            result_json = {
    82→                "status": "success",
    83→                "columns": result["columns"],
    84→                "rows": truncated_rows,
    85→                "rowCount": result["rowCount"],
    86→            }
    87→            return [types.TextContent(type="text", text=json.dumps(result_json, default=str))]
    88→        except Exception as e:
    89→            error_json = {"status": "error", "error": str(e)}
    90→            return [types.TextContent(type="text", text=json.dumps(error_json))]
    91→
    92→    return server
    93→
    94→
    95→async def handle_sse(request: Request) -> Response:
    96→    """Handle SSE connection. Requires session_id query param."""
    97→    session_id = request.query_params.get("session_id")
    98→    if not session_id:
    99→        return Response("session_id query parameter is required", status_code=400)
   100→
   101→    db = session_manager.get_or_create(session_id)
   102→    server = _create_mcp_server(db)
   103→
   104→    async with sse_transport.connect_sse(
   105→        request.scope, request.receive, request._send
   106→    ) as streams:
   107→        await server.run(
   108→            streams[0],
   109→            streams[1],
   110→            server.create_initialization_options(),
   111→        )
   112→
   113→    return Response()
   114→
   115→
   116→mcp_app = Starlette(
   117→    routes=[
   118→        Route("/sse", endpoint=handle_sse, methods=["GET"]),
   119→        Mount("/messages/", app=sse_transport.handle_post_message),
   120→    ]
   121→)
   122→```
   123→
   124→Key details:
   125→- Reuses `session_manager.get_or_create()` (from `session_manager.py`) for DuckDB lookup
   126→- Replicates the same `execute_sql` logic from `tools.py` (truncate to 100 rows, return JSON)
   127→- `SseServerTransport("/mcp/messages/")` — the `/mcp` prefix comes from the mount point in `main.py`
   128→- One `SseServerTransport` instance shared across sessions (it handles internal session routing via UUID)
   129→- Each SSE connection creates a fresh `MCPServer` bound to the session's DuckDB
   130→
   131→### Task 2: Mount MCP app in `backend/app/main.py`
   132→
   133→Add one line to mount the Starlette app:

   136→from app.mcp_sse import mcp_app

   152→    "mcp_server_url": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/mcp/sse?session_id={container_session}",
   153→}
   154→```
   155→
   156→### Task 4: Write tests for `backend/tests/test_mcp_sse.py`
   157→
   158→Test:
   159→- `_create_mcp_server` returns a server with `execute_sql` tool
   160→- `handle_sse` returns 400 when `session_id` is missing
   161→- The tool correctly executes SQL against a DuckDB instance
   162→- The tool handles SQL errors gracefully
